Within-batch counts that are fixed: the churn figure was 126 on **day 18**, the Wednesday before Friday's 131 on day 20; the wells went off on the twenty-first, twenty-third, twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth; Slee's report reached the district on the twenty-fourth and the notice was served on the twenty-eighth; the run was cut on the twenty-second and the water let in at half past four; the bleed stopped before the second hour of the twenty-seventh; the three acres began to go on the Sunday, lay over on the Monday and Tuesday, and came up sour on the Friday.

**The month boundary, and how to date a day. Months run thirty days, and season day 1 is the first of October; season day 31 is therefore the first of the next month, and season day 61 the first of the month after that.** Days 1–20 are the tail of October and days 21–30 its head, so **every day in 21–30 is the twenty-first … the thirtieth of October and nothing else.** A later writer must observe this:

| Season day | How the prose may date it |
|---|---|
| 1–20 (October) | the plain day number — *the tenth*, *the seventeenth* — or **of last month**, or **of this season**. **Never *of this month*.** |
| 21–30 (October) | **the plain number only** — *the twenty-first*, the twenty-second, the twenty-third, the twenty-fourth, the thirtieth — or **of last month** once the prose has passed the month end. **Never the first, second, third … of a month, and never *of this month* after day 30.** |
| 31–40 | the ordinal of the new month (*the first*, the second, the third … the tenth) or **of this month** |
| 41+ | the ordinal (*the eleventh* … the twentieth) or the plain season day number (*the forty-second*, *the fifty-first*) |

**This table was wrong until the Batch 0004 review pass and is now the governing rule.** The earlier version said *season day 21 is the first of a calendar month*, which made the month twenty days long and could not be reconciled with the batch's own forward dates; three in-text facts decide it and all three agree: **Ch 25 says the first of next month is *six days* away on day 25; Ch 40 says the first of next month is *twenty-one days* away on day 40; and Ch 34's fourteen-day notice served on day 34 makes an independent reading possible *about the eighteenth*, which is day 48.** All three are true only if day 31 is the first of a month. See § *Batch 0004 day clock* and § *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0004 on review*, item 1.

**The form is due in eleven working days from the twenty-eighth, which is season day 42, and the sitting has not been appointed.** A later writer should place Movement 4's deadline in those terms and not in a new month name.

### Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0003

- **Two contacts behind him, unchanged.** No third, no rootmark, no anchor work, no bridge above local conduit, no seedheart, no caretaker link. Marek **offers his own body as a return substitution in Ch 29 and is refused on the page**, on two grounds: a practitioner is Stage 0 with two contacts he cannot repeat, and a body is a return, not a source. **That is the only Stage 1 flirtation in the batch and it was not taken.**
- Sera has checked his arm four times — the eighth, the twelfth, the twentieth, the thirtieth — and it is still nothing.
- **One Fieldbook panel in the batch**, in Ch 24, three plain lines, at the moment of the choice about the run, in the book, in a licensed reader's hands, read aloud to four witnesses.
- **Sera has read the node four times in the volume**: the eighth (maintenance sequence plus the shaft), the twentieth (*cut, wait, cut*), and once in this batch (the three lines). Her Ch 20 sequence is untouched and is still one step short of the Ch 46 answer.
- **Marek has not touched living wood since the tenth of last month.** He puts his hand on the Willow stake's willow on the thirtieth and takes it off, in the prose, because he is the engineer of record and there is nobody licensed standing next to him. Do not give him another.
- **Renn Tallow's gate refusal has now been twenty days old and is not resolved, referred to, softened, or apologized for.** He asks for it to be recorded and nobody records it.

### New locks from the drafted Batch 0004

- **The batch's one Fieldbook panel was not used.** The instrument was never opened in the batch — Bray's application failed on procedure, the hearing officer ruled that nobody in the room may open it, and it is still in the press under the ledger. **One panel remains unspent in the volume, and the next writer may spend it once, at a moment of choice, three plain lines.** Nothing in Chapters 31–40 may be quoted by any document as an instruction, and Sera Quill's unnumbered fourth refusal (a book does not get to be a reason) stands untouched.
- **The month boundary is now right in the prose, and the earlier claim that the rule held without a single violation was false.** Days 31–40 are the first to the tenth of the month after October, and every one of them is dated by its ordinal or its plain season day number. Days 1–30 of October are dated *of last month* or by their plain number, never *of this month*; **the Batch 0004 review found roughly a dozen places in Chs 21–30 where days 21–30 were still called the first … the tenth of a month, the direct consequence of the retired day-21 lock, and all of them are now corrected in the prose.** **October appears once in the batch, in speech, in Ch 34, and is the month the flock went up the fen road on the tenth, as established.** February appears **five** times — Ch 31 once, Ch 33 twice, Ch 39 once, Ch 40 once — all as a month ahead. No document, notice, form, circular, caution, license sheet or letter in Chapters 31–40 carries a month name.
- **The cheap reed gauge at the eleventh rod has a fixed history and it is a hole, not a practice.** It was clamped on the return main on **the twenty-first of last month** (Ch 23) and Jonas Byre read it that same morning and **wrote nothing down**, and the entry that exists says in its own words that the reading for that morning cannot be supplied. **Nobody in this batch began a daily practice and nobody has read and signed it since.** Ch 40's plan is that Jonas, Gael and Ilsa read it at the fourth hour on day 41, write it, and enter the load under it. **A later writer may not say the gauge has been read every morning since the twenty-first, and may not put a number in any book for it before day 41.**
- **Sera Quill is not asked about Brinewake in this batch and does not offer it.** She is named on the Assembly's circular and nowhere else. Nobody in Tarrow Fen has ever seen Halden Pike and nobody in this batch asked to.
- **Gael Senn's monthly examination is a document that records a thing that did not happen, and that is now the building's rule.** *Nothing found* remains the wording for the wound, and the return line has its own wording for an absence. Nobody may collapse the two. And she has **declined to write an entry in advance**, on the ground that it would be the same rule as *nothing found*; a later writer must not have her fill the log speculatively.
- **The certificate's return is two things and not one**, and the second thing cannot be performed for want of a hundredweight. This is not a new rule; it is what the certificate said from the twentieth of last month and it has now failed in public once.
- **The seal is untouched.** A quarter of an inch, measured twice with two rules, pump on the idle, no change since the thirtieth of last month, and the load stated has not been applied. **Half an inch is still where Sera stops calling it a weep**, and a leak under sacking at full draw still goes into the housing and not into the pit. Do not resolve before Ch 41 and do not let it be quietly mended.
- **The pressure pulse is now logged seven times**, always as *cause not established*, always with no name, and the seventh entry is the first one made in a book with **no licensed person present** — which is the closest anybody in the volume has come to making the gap in this cooperative's records visible, and nobody in the batch says so out loud. Nobody in Volume 01 may name or explain it.
- **The three exchange requirements are named for the burn and they do not come out better.** The windbreak's root line is the anchor and the path; the return is a thing nobody has, because a fire takes it. Sera Quill will not license it, and she is the only licensed engineer in the district, and a Crown operation does not need her license. **The third exchange requirement is still missing on the one licensed work in the volume and a later writer may not quietly fill it.**
- **The bypass is unbuilt and stays unbuilt through Ch 41.** It was refused on the twenty-seventh of last month on five correct grounds, offered free on the sixth of this month and refused on five correct grounds, and the four men who offered to dig it came up the lane on the night of the ninth and were not asked in. There is now a **fourth** section-nine margin note, in the same hand on the same page, closing with the same sentence. Dunmar Fitch offered to vary the closing sentence for a flood and was refused. **Do not build it, fund it, or have it appear.**
- **Dunmar's *none known* is spent.** It was stated, with a witness, at the Fennmere counter on the seventh of this month, and deliberately kept shut, on the ground that a clerk who files a false statement cannot testify to the four true documents in the same file. It must not be spent a second time. **What survives instead is the statement he made while spending it**, which is now in the district's register: a clerk of an applicant is not a witness and is not offering to be one.
- **The open book is still not a filing, and this is now stated in a district register.** Dunmar Fitch said it at the counter on the seventh, with the nine-to-twenty-two vote and the fact that there has never been a corrected figure in the book, and Perrin Osse entered it. **No clerk, hearing officer or officer may treat the cooperative's books as an accepted correction.**
- **Perrin Osse's statement about the works schedule is unchanged and was reinforced in Ch 37**, where she entered that a district may certify a fact about its own completion record — three stamped faces against N-2214/K — without producing a page of the table. **The table itself is still never produced and the Crown-held column is still the reason.** Nobody hid anything. Do not let a villain reverse this.
- **Bray was moved by a document and the document was the cooperative's own honest record, and it moved him against them, which is the cruelty of the movement.** His application failed. He did not withdraw it, he said the record is the best-maintained of its kind he has read in twenty-two years, and he said the sentence: *your honesty made this possible and it is going to be used, and I would rather it were me using it than a hearing officer finding it on her own.* He is not outsmartable and he is not friendly.
- **The instrument is protected and the clean record is gone, and both are true at once.** Lissa Vale won on a form and a lock and lost the last account of her husband's fieldbook that nobody else had ever held. **She is not sorry and she has said so, and a later writer must not make her sorry and must not make her be thanked.**
- **The district now holds: a certified copy of the standing pressure log that cannot be recalled and can never be added to without a district officer present; the cooperative's own margin note of the twenty-seventh, as the disclosed source of the third stamped face; the three lines of the twenty-fourth of last month in a clerk's square hand; a copy of the statement of the thirtieth; the use log's first line, produced on a form and minuted with the hour; and the cooperative's lateness in entering it, declared by Lissa Vale before anybody found it.** None of this is the works schedule.
- **A hearing officer exists and is named: Hester Carrow**, and the sitting is **the twenty-first of this month**. The hearing is in Chapters 46–48; this is only the date and the officer, and the officer has ruled that nobody may open the instrument in her room and that the ruling binds her successors.
- **The fieldbook will be produced on the twenty-first and not before.** Lissa Vale has committed to it in writing in front of the officer. **This is the plant for the volume climax, where the use log is read aloud at the water's edge: the log is already in a district record, and Lissa is bringing it to a cold room anyway, and she has spent an afternoon teaching the officer what is in it so that the reading of the tenth is the second time and not the first.** A later writer may have the production happen early because of the water; the officer's own sentence — *a copy of a book can be produced in a room where the book is not, and a book out of a press in a fen in this season is a book a clerk should not be asked to carry up a road* — is on the table and should be used.
- **The second reader is now a live obligation rather than an impossibility.** The notice is served, the envelope is sealed under Lissa's name in the press, and the earliest possible reading is about the eighteenth of this month, against a price of fourteen pounds ten and a box of four pounds five. **A reading is possible and cannot be bought.** That is the shape of the last third of the volume.
- **The Thornwild refusal is recorded as a fact with no terms in it, and its terms are not held by this body.** A later writer may not have the cooperative use the long line, quote it, or enter it, and may not have any character ask Mear Sallow's settlement to be named. She is not in the book, the settlement is not in the book, and the one word *seat* was refused.
- **The Undercommons' four men are not a rescue and are not coming back for Lantern Reach.** They were not asked in on the night of the ninth and went on down the fen road, and nobody in the yard knows what they did. **Whether they appear again in Movement 5 is open and belongs to the flood chapters; do not bring them back to dig a bypass.** The pathhound was reported at the gate and nothing was written underneath it. It is not a symbol and nobody in the batch interpreted it.
- **The burn is not agreed and not refused.** The offer lapses unanswered on the sixth, a shortened measure would require a new form on a date nobody has, and two members of the body have gone to a district counter on their own account to ask about it. **The crew is split at the end of the batch and is not healed.** A later writer may resolve it during the flood or may not.
- **The storm is forecast, not arrived.** The caution gives rain on the main drain for three days **from the twelfth day of this month** and a rise in the low quarter on the second night, and the chapter closes on a cheap and competent act of preparation: read the reed gauge at the eleventh rod at the fourth hour, write the load under it, and be on the embankment at the low corner at the first hour with bars and boards. **The flood is Chapters 41–46.**
- **Marek is Stage 0 and has not touched living wood.** He has had no third contact. He offered his body as a return in Ch 29 and was refused and has not offered it again. He put his hand on nothing in this batch and there is no rootmark. **There was no licensed engineer in the fen on the first of the month, when the second examination was kept, and Sera Quill came up the road on the four o'clock cart on the third and is in the yard on the third and the sixth, and does not go near the pit.** See the correction recorded under *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0004 on review*.
- **Nia is twenty days gone at the end of the batch and does not return.** Her letter is still in a cart. **Neither Nia, nor Kellan, nor the letter is named anywhere in Chapters 31–40.** The only adjacent beat is Tova Reed's refusal in Ch 39 to say whose hand the March's copy goes into, and nobody asks. Whether she hands the letter over is still unsettled and must not be settled by report.
- **Renn Tallow does not appear in this batch and is not referred to.** Kellan Rusk does not appear and has not been heard from for eighteen days.
- **Nothing romantic happened.** Tova Reed and Marek have each refused the other in a business document and signed a contract on the same day. Neither is rescued, neither is softened, and the trust's name on the record is the test and the test is institutional.

### Corrections the writer made to the drafted Batch 0004 (a self-check, not a review pass)

**Provenance, and read this before trusting any number in the list below.** This list was originally headed *Fixes applied to the drafted Batch 0004 on review* and opened by saying a review pass had been run and its findings applied. **That was false, and the Batch 0004 review said so.** There was no `reviews/batch-0004.findings.md` and no review-fix commit; the twenty items below were the **writer's own corrections to its own draft while drafting**, and the writer wrote its own audit trail for them. The independent review of this batch is a separate, later event, recorded in the next section. **The corrections themselves are real and are still in the prose** — a spot-check of seven of them found them genuinely applied — but a later writer must not read this list as a reviewer's findings, and the review's own report is at `reviews/batch-0004.findings.md`.

The batch was **not** restarted, no scene was replaced, and no planned plot was changed: the liability landing on paper, Halden Pike's circular, the burn offer and the crew split, the second reader sought, the Thornwild refusal, the Undercommons offer, the certified copy, the seizure and the loss of the clean record, the seed annex and the storm forecast are all as drafted. **Three of the twenty are themselves wrong and were corrected on the review pass** — items 2, 6 and 8 below reasoned from a month boundary that does not hold, and their conclusions have been re-derived from the correct one.

1. **The compost arithmetic was wrong by a factor of seventeen in Ch 31.** £4 5s is 85 shillings and compost is 4 s a hundredweight, so it is **twenty-one hundredweight and a quarter**, and twenty-one and a quarter hundredweight out of four hundred is **about a twentieth of one month's return**, not a quarter. The "quarter of one month's return" belongs to one hundredweight and is locked in Ch 28. Both the chapter and the summary now say a twentieth, and Gael Senn says out loud that it is not nothing, because the emotional point of the passage depends on the figure being true.
2. **Ch 37 mis-dated and mis-timed the thirtieth's statement.** Ch 30 is day 30, which is **the thirtieth, the last day of October**, and the statement went in **at the ninth hour**; the copy has **the thirtieth of last month at the ninth hour**. The *seventh hour and twenty minutes* in the first draft was the Ch 19 use-log figure. This is a district register and a clerk could check it. *(This item originally reasoned that day 30 is "the tenth of the first month". The date it arrived at was right and the reasoning was not; see the review pass below, item 1.)*
3. **Ch 37's certification was internally impossible.** The prose called it *six words* and then quoted a forty-five-word certification dated at the seventh hour, when the copy stopped at half past three and the certification was made at four. The quoted certification now reads **the fourth hour of the afternoon**; "six words" is gone from Ch 37, and Ch 38's echo of it is now **five words at the foot of a certificate**, which is what *so far as it goes* is — **and the second audit finished the job, because five further chapters still called a five-word phrase six words (Chs 37, 38, 41, 44 and 47) while Ch 38 already said five**.
4. **Ch 37 contained a metafictional intrusion**, *the least dramatic thing in this volume*, of the same class as the Ch 18 and Ch 28 intrusions. Now **in this business**.
5. **Ch 37 attributed the Weir Row exchange to the wrong man.** In Ch 27 the man with the board and the clip reads the item number, and the man with the mallet says *you are welcome to stand there*. The attribution is corrected, and the whole exchange is now in a district register as the cooperative's account of its own silence, which is why it matters.
6. ~~**Ch 38 put the fourth of the month in the wrong calendar month.** Ch 24 is day 24, which is **the fourth of the first month**, so from the eighth of the second month it is **the fourth day of last month**.~~ **This item was wrong and it is the one that caused the calendar defect.** Ch 24 is day 24 and the licensed reading is **the twenty-fourth**, not the fourth of anything. Ch 38's *the fourth day of last month* has been corrected to **the twenty-fourth day of last month**, and this item's reasoning is withdrawn. **A later writer must say the twenty-first … the thirtieth for every day in 21–30, and *of last month* for all of them once the prose has passed day 30.**
7. **Ch 38's fifty minutes were not fifty minutes.** The use-log line reads *out at the seventh hour and ten minutes, back at the eighth hour of the day*, which is fifty minutes; Lissa's gloss said *eight in the evening*, which is twelve hours fifty. Now **eight in the morning**.
8. **Ch 33 put a Movement 3 date in the wrong calendar month.** Sera Quill licensed the run on **the twenty-first and twenty-second of last month**, not *in this month*, and Anneke Roos's *both in the same month* became **both put in front of a district in a month**, because the consent and the license sheet are a month apart. *(The date in this item was itself restated on the review pass, which is what found the month boundary wrong.)*
9. **Ch 31 carried a stale count and a paragraph cloned from Ch 23.** *Three figures* became **five**, and the paragraph was rewritten in Ch 31's own words around the news that is actually new: **254 is a flow and 181 is a man turning a wheel with his own arm because there is no water, and a hearing officer is entitled to be told which is which.** The *stood with the pen in his hand afterwards* shape, which Ch 23 had already used, is gone.
10. **Ch 31's countdown was wrong**: nine days became **eleven**, to the forty-second.
11. **The state files claimed there was no licensed engineer in the fen for the whole of the batch, and that was false.** Sera Quill comes up the road on the four o'clock cart on the third and is in the yard on the **third and the sixth**; she is in Ch 33 and Ch 36 and refuses the burn and the Undercommons' ditch with the same sentence, deliberately. **Ch 33 now says why she is there** — she has come to close the certificate out, take her papers, put the fee on an invoice and go, and she says on the cart that she is not going near that pit — **and Ch 31's statement of why the full-draw load is not applied has been rewritten to rest on the load test being the twentieth day's act on a certificate with no charter to hang a second one on.** That is a stronger lock than the one it replaces, because it is a reason rather than an absence, and **a licensed engineer is in the fen again from Ch 41 onward, which matters.** `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `bible/characters.md` were all corrected. **The weep remains a quarter of an inch at the idle, unmeasured at load, and untouched.**
12. **Ch 36 numbered Sera Quill's refusals again** — *I will not say it a fifth time in a season* — which is the exact error the Batch 0003 review removed from Ch 30. The numbering is gone; the line now says she has said it before this month, in this yard, on a different morning, to the same four people, and will not say it differently for a better reason. **No refusal of hers is numbered anywhere in Chapters 31–40.**
13. **Both mill-board notes carried the wrong weekday.** The first of the month is a **Tuesday** and the ninth is a **Wednesday**. The two rows in the table above were wrong in the same way and are corrected.
14. **Ch 40 said twenty-one days and then twenty-three for the same date.** Both are **twenty-one**, from the tenth of the month to the first of the next.
15. **The seed annex could not have taken two days.** The crates arrive on the ninth in the middle of the morning and the annex is signed the same afternoon, so it is **one day**, and the *first afternoon / second afternoon* structure became *in the annexe that afternoon / what was left of the day*.
16. **Ch 35 costed a stranger's supper on the wrong day** — the eighth for the fifth — and the same sentence had the mill floor warm because the mill wheel was not turning, which is backwards. The fifth, and a cold floor because the wheel has not turned since the twenty-fourth.
17. **Ch 35's countdown was wrong**: in eleven days became **in seven days**, from the fifth to the forty-second.
18. **Ch 32 put a notice on the well house door that did not exist yet.** The caution does not arrive until the tenth. The door carries **two** things on the second, not three, and Ilsa Kern's line now forecasts the third rather than counting it.
19. **Ch 38 gave Lissa Vale another woman's sentence, another woman's day, and another woman's witness.** The proof sentence is **Sera Quill's**, spoken on the thirtieth of last month, and the witness to the four seconds on the night of the tenth was **Sera Quill**, not the custodian. Both of Lissa's speeches are rewritten: she now quotes the sentence as a thing written in her own body's book in a licensed engineer's hand, names Sera as its author and the thirtieth as its date, and then makes **her own claim, which is that she is a woman who was in the room at a counter on the tenth holding a different book and did not see what her son got**, and refuses a third time in front of the hearing officer. **The founding sentence and its witness stay with Sera Quill.**
20. **Ch 34's sealed envelope existed before it was sealed.** Three passages contradicted each other. The thing in Marek's coat is now **a sheet of seed-house paper folded twice with three lines on it in his own hand, written at his mother's table at six in the morning before he left the gate**, and Lissa Vale folds and seals it that evening in front of nobody.

The non-blocking items in the same self-check were also applied. Ch 37's *the district read it out loud at a counter on Saturday* is softened, because Bray did not read the windbreak entry out (item 21). Ch 33's *Ms Quill's license sheet is in a district file* becomes **an extract** of it is in a district file, because the sheet itself is not filed anywhere and never has been (22). **The March's tin went into the press on the nineteenth of last month, not the twentieth, so it has been at risk twenty days, not nineteen**, and the table and the summary are corrected (23). Ch 37's *so far as it goes* explanation was stating the opposite of the lock and is corrected: a page going into the original means a difference has to be **reconciled in the copy, and only Perrin Osse may do it, from the original, with the custodian present** (24). The hearing officer's closing line no longer says the application and the schedule fall due in the same week, because the sitting is the twenty-first and the money is payable on the forty-second; she now says **the money falls due before the sitting** (25). *A month later* and *in six weeks there is a hearing* in Ch 34 became **eleven days later** and **in a month** (26, 27). Ch 37's *three weeks* in the tray became **thirteen days** (28); Ch 38's *three days being unable to look at it* became **a night** (29), *three weeks* in the yard became **four weeks** (30), and *the second page was nearly empty* became **the first page, because there have only ever been two lines in the whole of it** (31). A counted *fourth time in a month* in Ch 31 was dropped rather than renumbered (32). A garbled sentence about four crates in Ch 31 was rewritten around the open compost crate (33). Ch 32's date on the shortfall line was dropped in favor of *at the head of a license sheet this body has never filed anywhere* (34). **A fen that burns in the third week** became **the second week**, because the measure is the sixth to the twelfth (35). Ch 35's *arguing since Wednesday* became **since yesterday afternoon** (36), *noticed it from nine miles* became **from nine days** (37), and a typo in Ch 36 — *a such a place* — is fixed (38). Ch 38's simile about Sera Quill's nine pages was unplaceable and is now **Dunmar Fitch recognising, in a hearing officer's own gesture, the thing Sera Quill did to a seed house table in the first week of last month** (39). Repetition was reduced where the self-check found a genuine tic rather than a register (40). **Two state-file claims were overclaims and are corrected**: the letter is not mentioned anywhere in Chapters 31–40, and the only adjacent beat is Tova Reed's refusal in Ch 39 to say whose hand the March's copy goes into (41). A paraphrase of the willow stake's line was aligned with Ch 30's *alive in February* (42). **The list was renumbered on the review pass: it ran 21–43 with no 38, and it is now continuous.**

**One consequence the drafting surfaced that is not a fix and is recorded here for Batch 0005.** The pathhound report of the sixth of the month is entered in the **standing pressure log**, not the use log, and Ch 37 certifies that log to the district and Ch 39 sends a copy to Cray. **So the report that there is an old collie standing in the dead channel at the bottom of the low quarter, and that a ditch would move it, is now in three places: the open book, a certified copy in a Fennmere file, and a copy going to the March road.** Nothing in the batch interpreted it and nobody may, but a later writer should know that the fact has left the yard.

**One more thing recorded for Batch 0005.** The sitting is **the twenty-first of the month, which is season day 51**, so Chapters 41–49 must reach eleven days past the last day of this batch. And **a licensed engineer is in the fen again from Ch 33**, which means the excuse for not applying a full-draw load to the seal is no longer her absence; it is that the load test was the twentieth day's act on a certificate and there is no charter to hang a second one on. **That reason has to survive into Ch 41.**

### The Batch 0004 review pass — independent, and what it changed

An independent review of Batch 0004 was run after the batch was drafted. Its report is `logs/batch-0004.review.log` and its findings are transcribed at **`reviews/batch-0004.findings.md`**. It is the first genuine review of this batch; the list above is the writer's own. It returned **three blocking findings and six non-blocking plus a length note**, and all of them have been applied on this fix pass. **The batch was not restarted, no scene was replaced, and no planned plot moved.** The refusal ladder, the seizure, the loss of the clean record, the seed annex and the storm forecast are as drafted. The review's closing judgment is worth keeping: the batch is strong prose and it was not shippable as recorded, and the reason was in the state files rather than in the chapters.

1. **BLOCKING — the manuscript held two mutually exclusive calendars, and the state file certified the one the chapters broke.** The day clock asserted both *day 21 is the first of a calendar month* and *days 31–40 are the first through the tenth of the second calendar month* — a twenty-day month and a thirty-day month. **Resolved by adopting the thirty-day month, which the batch's own prose already required, and retiring the day-21 lock.** Three in-text facts decide it: Ch 25's *six days*, Ch 40's *twenty-one days*, and Ch 34's fourteen-day notice making a reading possible *about the eighteenth*. **The prose was then repaired throughout**, not only in the two phrases the review named: days 21–30 had been called the first … the tenth of a month in **Chs 21, 22, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29 and 30**, including five quoted records that had to move together — the Ch 24 use-log line and Sera's dictation, the entry Bray reads out in Ch 28, Teale's two board letters in Chs 23 and 24, and the return-line record in Ch 24. **Ch 21's title was *The First Of The Month* and is now *Ten Days Early*, because the examination in it is no longer the certificate's monthly return: on day 21 Gael Senn goes down the ladder ten days early because her name is second on the certificate and nobody else would go, and the certificate's first monthly return is the first of the month, which is day 31.** The certificate's own requirement — an examination *and* something put into the return main, on the first of every month — is unchanged and is what Ch 31 and Ch 40 are built on. The vote that lost nine to twenty-two was on **the fourth**, not the third, and is corrected in Chs 30, 37 and 39; the coupling that spoiled three trays was on **the third**, and Ch 39's reference to it is unchanged.
2. **BLOCKING — the state files certified a review that did not exist.** Fixed by this section, by the rewritten header above, and by the new `reviews/batch-0004.findings.md`. **Nothing else in the batch was affected: the seven corrections the review spot-checked are genuinely in the prose.**
3. **BLOCKING — Ch 40 mis-dated the reed gauge and silently reversed Ch 23.** Ch 23 puts the cheap reed gauge on the return main at the eleventh rod, and its cost is that Jonas Byre read it and wrote nothing down. Ch 40 had it on the peg *since the fourth of the month* with a daily reading-and-signing practice that no chapter shows beginning. **Ch 40 now dates it from the twenty-first of last month, the day it was actually clamped, and says plainly that Jonas read it that morning, wrote nothing, and that the one entry which exists says the reading for that morning cannot be supplied.** The gauge's history is now a lock (see *New locks from the drafted Batch 0004*), and **Ch 23's *on the Sunday* is corrected to *on the Saturday***, which is the day Jonas's speech already says he read it.
4. **Ch 35 dated the yard's argument to the wrong weekday.** The form was read out and argued for five hours on **the third**, not on Wednesday; Wednesday is the second, the day the circular came. Both instances corrected. **The same chapter's posting-day was also wrong**: Mear Sallow said the district's man posted the measure on *Tuesday*, which is the day before the measure existed — it is issued on the third — and it is now **Thursday**, with the district's man at the Assemtry's gate on **the Friday**.
5. **Ch 35 merged two forms' features.** *The second issue of a form and there is a stamp on it* belonged to the Assembly circular A/4; the hazard measure EM/2 has a form number and no stamp, and Mear Sallow now says so, and says why the difference matters.
6. **February appears five times in the batch, not twice** — Ch 31 once, Ch 33 twice, Ch 39 once, Ch 40 once, all as a month ahead. October appears once, in speech, in Ch 34, and the rest of the row is corrected: the earlier claim that the month-boundary rule held without a single violation was false.
7. **`state/current.md:45` was garbled** — a capital after a comma and a lost clause between two facts — and **`current.md:33` contradicted itself in one sentence** about *none known* being both spent and deliberately kept shut. Both repaired. The word is now **left shut**: he stated the history of the box on the seventh and did not fill it in.
8. **Length.** 29,743 words, +6.2% on the 28,000 target, the smallest overrun of the volume — but only Ch 32 and Ch 34 sit under the 2,600–2,900 band, and Ch 38 at 3,732 is the one permitted exception. **Recorded in `state/current.md` with the whole account, because Movement 5 is nine chapters carrying the flood, the proof, the hearing and the resolution and cannot afford a 3,000-word house average.** This is a note to the next writer, not a defect in these chapters.
9. **What the review says to preserve.** The refusal ladder in Chs 35–36 — five people refusing for five different *correct* reasons, no chorus, no vote; Lissa winning on procedure and losing the clean record and saying she is not sorry; the instrument surviving unopened; Bray moved by the cooperative's own record against them and not outsmartable. **It also checked the numbers it could check**: 85s ÷ 4s = 21¼ cwt = a twentieth of 400; £80 a month; sixteen households against 35 = 9 + 22 + 4; N-2214/K consistent across Chs 7, 8, 15, 16, 20 and 26; thirteen days from the twenty-seventh to the tenth; twenty days the tin has been at risk. **All four are re-verified under the thirty-day month, since three of them cross the boundary.**

### Batch 0004 day clock (confirmed against the drafted chapters)

Ch 31 day 31 (Tue, **the first of a calendar month**) · Ch 32 day 32 (Wed, the second) · Ch 33 day 33 (Thu, the third) · Ch 34 day 34 (Fri, the fourth) · Ch 35 day 35 (Sat, the fifth) · Ch 36 day 36 (Sun, the sixth) · Ch 37 day 37 (Mon, the seventh) · Ch 38 day 38 (Tue, the eighth) · Ch 39 day 39 (Wed, the ninth) · Ch 40 day 40 (Thu, the tenth).

**Days 1–30 are October and day 31 is the first of the month after it.** The earlier version of this clock carried a second, incompatible lock — that day 21 was the first of a calendar month — and the two could not both be true, because the first makes the month twenty days long and the second thirty. **The thirty-day reading is now the only one, and the batch's prose already required it**: on day 25 the first of the month is *six days* away (Ch 25), on day 40 the first of next month is *twenty-one days* away (Ch 40), and a notice served on day 34 running fourteen days makes an independent reading possible *about the eighteenth*, which is day 48 (Ch 34). The day numbers and the weekdays are untouched by this; **what changed is that days 21–30 are the twenty-first … the thirtieth of October and are no longer the first … the tenth of anything.** Every document in the batch that carries a day carries one of these ten numbers, or a number in 1–30 belonging to October, which from day 31 onward is *last month*.

Counts inside the batch that are fixed and may be checked: the return line records the last compost as **the twenty-first of last month**; the weep is unchanged **since the thirtieth of last month**; the schedule of damage was served **the twenty-eighth of last month** (day 28) and the notice of received report on the twenty-fourth; the third cut was **the twenty-seventh of last month** and the bypass was refused the same morning; the licensed reading is **the twenty-fourth of last month at the tenth hour and forty minutes**; the use log's first line is **the tenth of last month** and the book was made on the eleventh; the cooperative's own motion to enter the claim in the log was **lost on the fourth** by nine to twenty-two with four abstentions; the bypass was offered on **the sixth of this month**; the four men came on **the night of the ninth**; the caution gives rain **from the twelfth of this month**; the sitting is **the twenty-first of this month**, which is season day 51; the representations and the sixty pounds ten fall due **in or about the forty-second**; the March's tin has been in the press **twenty days**, it having gone in on the nineteenth of last month; and the first of next month is **twenty-one days** from the last day of this batch.

### Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0004

- **Two contacts behind him, unchanged.** No third, no rootmark, no anchor work, no bridge above local conduit, no seedheart, no caretaker link. He has not touched living wood since the night of the tenth of last month. **There is no licensed engineer in the fen on the first of the month, which is entered in the log twice, and one arrives on the third and one leaves on the sixth.**
- Sera has checked his arm four times — the eighth, the twelfth, the twentieth, the thirtieth of last month — and it is still nothing. **She is in the yard on the third and the sixth of this month and does not check it a fifth time and does not go near the pit.** The *twice a week* instruction remains spent and must not be said again.
- **No Fieldbook panel in the batch.** One remains in the volume.
- **Marda Vole does not appear** and is not referred to. The Undercommons in this volume is now **two men**: one courier with a warning and a piece of procedure in Ch 15, and one offerer with an obligation in Ch 36, and **neither is a rescue and neither returns**.

### Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Batch 0004

Batch 0004 added seven small things by drafting. All are local detail, mechanisms, or people, and none changes a world rule:

1. **Mear Sallow**, a Thornwild courier, and the **Assemtry**, a settlement of about ninety people with no charter, no seat and no name on any roll. Added because the movement requires a refusal made on behalf of somebody with no standing, and a refusal needs a person.
2. **Ivo Tarrant**, a peat cutter, who offers four men and an obligation. Added because the Undercommons is not a rescue and has to be worse than rescue, and because somebody has to be the reason the bypass stays unbuilt for a good reason.
3. **Hester Carrow**, appointed hearing officer for the Tarrow district, and **the sitting on the twenty-first of the month.** Added because the volume's deciding forum needed a name, a face and a date, and because the fieldbook's production had to be ordered by somebody.
4. **The Assembly circular, form A/4**, and the **hazard measure, form EM/2**, and the **caution, form C/2**, and the **water-supply bylaw's second part** (cautions) alongside its established sixth part (schedule of damage). All are district or professional instruments and none is to be confused with anything later.
5. **The certified copy of the standing pressure log and the phrase *so far as it goes*.** A district mechanism, and the batch's hinge: it cures half the cooperative's problem, prices it, and arms the seizure attempt in the next chapter.
6. **The seed annex**, two sheets, with the Dryfoot Trust as a named party, and **the three-and-a-half-degree bed.** A contract, not a rule.
7. **The fourteen pounds ten day rate** for an independent licensed reading with an attested copy. A price, not a power rule, and it is the wall the volume climbs in Ch 44–46.

### Proposed canon changes recorded, not silently applied — Batch 0003

Batch 0003 added five small things by drafting. All are local detail, mechanisms, or people, and none changes a world rule:

1. **Rowan Teale**, the miller at Fennmere, who owns the board and reads it thirty-one times a day. Added because the mill tally is one of the four records at the climax and the cooperative cannot read its own most important instrument.
2. **Corvin Slee**, of the low quarter, whose report of the twenty-fourth reopened the file. His wells, his pail, and his bucket are the batch's human cost and his is the document that closed the last door.
3. **Joss Pyle**, the boy in the low quarter with the collie. Eleven. No dialogue beyond three lines. Do not give him a second chapter.
4. **Idris Vane**, 61, the second name on the Assembly's roll for Tarrow district, the reader the proof rule needs. He has not appeared. He is the instrument the Ch 46 climax requires and he must be **asked for, paid for, and given notice** before he can be used.
5. **The district water-supply bylaw, sixth part, and the schedule of damage under it.** A form, a schedule, and a box marked *amount disputed*. It is a district instrument, not a Crown Engine mechanism, and it is never to be confused with anything later.

### Supporting cast drafted in Batch 0001
All five are now drafted and have been promoted into `bible/characters.md` as secondary characters: Ilsa Kern, Dunmar Fitch, Gael Senn, Ansel Bray, Renn Tallow. Perrin Osse (Fennmere sub-office clerk) and Bram Oskell (Kellan Rusk's papers) also read as drafted and were promoted. Fennmere remains a place, not a character.

### Supporting cast drafted in Batch 0002

Three new people read as drafted and are promoted into `bible/characters.md`: **Anneke Roos**, **Jonas Byre**, and **Marda Vole**. Two of the batch's existing minor characters have been given standing detail and are not new: **Lissa Vale's** rule of the fourteenth, and **Kellan Rusk's** compound book and Ch 18 letter.

**Renn Tallow does not appear in Batch 0002.** He is **named once**, in Ch 20, in a single line that says he was not there and had not been expected. *(The first pass wrote that he was not referred to anywhere in the batch; that was false, and the honest claim is that he is named once and does not appear.)* His refusal at the weir is not referred to, softened, or undone anywhere in the batch, and nobody makes anybody apologize for it.

### Supporting cast drafted in Batch 0003

**Rowan Teale** and **Corvin Slee** read as drafted and are promoted into `bible/characters.md`. **Joss Pyle** and **Idris Vane** are named once and once respectively; Joss Pyle is three lines and must stay three lines, and Idris Vane has not appeared and is introduced in the state files only, because the proof rule in Ch 30 has to name somebody who is real in order to be unsatisfiable.

The batch also gave standing detail to two existing characters and no new facts about the world: **Lissa Vale's one *no*** on the twenty-third, and **Sera Quill's fourth refusal**, which she does not number and does not explain.

### Supporting cast drafted in Batch 0004

**Four new people read as drafted and are promoted into `bible/characters.md`: Mear Sallow** (Thornwild courier, of the Assemtry, who refuses and gives the long line), **Ivo Tarrant** (peat cutter, who offers four men and an obligation), **Hester Carrow** (appointed hearing officer for the Tarrow district, who fixes the sitting), and **the Assemtry itself** (a settlement of about ninety with no charter, no seat and no name on a roll). **One existing character has been given a document and a price**: Idris Vane, who has a fieldbook of his own on his kitchen table that he does not offer and that Marek does not ask about, who is not refused and does not accept, and whose figure is **fourteen pounds ten**.

**Two figures who were named in the state files and had not appeared now appear properly and are promoted: Idris Vane** (see above) and, as a working instrument, **the miller's board as a record of a condition rather than a figure** — 181 turned by hand, and then *no wheel* — which is a third and fourth Movement 4 entry on the board and the first time in nineteen years a miller has written a line with no number on it.

**No new named member of the cooperative was created.** The crew of this movement is Ilsa Kern, Jonas Byre, Anneke Roos, Gael Senn, Lissa Vale and Dunmar Fitch, all of them already on the page, and **Ilsa Kern and Jonas Byre are the two who split off on their own account**, which is recorded as a fact about a body with no standing and not as a character fault.

## Ending lock

Iona's White Mercy is stopped through a braided, staged handoff to tested local seedhearts, not one overwhelming rootlight draw. Iona survives under public custody. The Engine becomes a bounded reservoir and archive, the Root Commons has local charters and vetoes, and some regions remain damaged or deliberately independent.
