**Two further corrections are not distances and are recorded here because they are facts.**

- **Tova Reed's tenure.** Ch 90 read *I have had it eleven months* of the stewardship. She has held it **eleven years**, and four other statements in this volume say so (Ch 72, Ch 91, Ch 96, Ch 97). *Eleven months* is an established house idiom for the length of this season and stands at Ch 88, 91, 92, 94 and 97; **it may not be used of a tenure.**
- **The levy.** The levy is on **sixteen households of this holding**, at four pounds five a quarter, three times, since Ch 18. Ch 90 twice said forty. The forty of Ch 80 is a hundredweight of compost coming off forty households and is left standing; **a later chapter may not put the eighteen pounds on forty households.**

**Also checked and found sound, and recorded so a later writer does not re-open them:** the tin at *day minus nineteen* unbroken (111 / 120 / 130); the bay boards at *day minus sixty-six* unbroken (71 on 137, 77 on 143, 79 on 145, 83 on 149); the seventh-column count 18 / 19 / 20 against the eleventh, the fifteenth and the twenty-third; the Longneck on the twenty-second of a month three months back, which is **day 52** and is correct where the batch prompt mislabels it; the reading rule of **day 80**; the Directorate reservation on **day 84**, the twenty-fourth of a month two months back, which is correct; the seed house's sixteen households and the tin at nine then eight; no month named anywhere; *seat* absent; none of the six forbidden names; no Fieldbook panel; no draw and no return by rootlight; straight quotation marks throughout and every multi-paragraph speech opening a mark in each paragraph and closing in the last; no duplicated paragraph in the batch. **One thing in the batch that looks like a defect and is not: the em dash at `chapter-0092.md:27` is the only one in the batch, and fifty-five chapters across both volumes use it, so it is this manuscript's idiom and not an intruder. A close pass must not flag it, and must not go through the volume stripping dashes on the strength of a single instance.**

**The batch is now 28,783 words by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their headings, range 2,620–3,103**, inside 27,000–30,000 and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. Ch 98 is seventeen words under the ceiling and **may not be lengthened.** *(The review pass took five words out of Ch 98 and the range is 2,620–3,103, not the 2,619–3,108 this line carried; the low figure was wrong by one before the review as well.)* **Volume 02 is therefore 141,818 words — 31,337 + 25,388 + 23,108 + 33,202 + 28,783 — and Volume 01's 166,022 stands.**

## Four places where the batch prompt itself is wrong, and the manuscript is right

**Recorded so that Volume 03 does not inherit them. None of the four was applied to the prose; in every case the prose already had it right.**

1. The prompt's day table row reading *the tenth of last month (119) → the tenth (130) = eleven days* mislabels day 119. Day 119 is **the twenty-ninth of last month**; the interval of eleven days is correct. The manuscript uses the right label throughout.
2. The prompt's locked day numbers label **day 4** *the fourth of a month three months back*. Day 4 is in days 1–30, which is **four months back** by day 130. Ch 89 says *four months back* and is right.
3. The same list labels **day 28** *the twenty-eighth of a month three months back*. Day 28 is also in days 1–30 and is **four months back**. No chapter in this batch dates day 28, so nothing was affected.
4. The same list labels **day 52** *the twenty-second of a month two months back*. Day 52 is in days 31–60 and is **three months back** by day 130. Ch 98 uses *three months back* and is right.
5. **A fifth, and the only one of the five the manuscript did NOT have right.** The prompt's day table row reading *the ninth of last month (129) → the twenty-ninth (149) = twenty* mislabels day 129, and **the verification pass above believed it and propagated it into four chapters.** Day 129 is **the ninth of this month**; day 99 is the ninth of last month. The prompt is internally contradictory on this — its *day clock* section and its *weekday* paragraph both say day 129 is the ninth of the month that began on day 121, and only the day table says *of last month*. **Chs 91, 94 and 96 dated the district's four points to the ninth of last month, and Ch 98's closing speech dated the district's answer to the ninth of last month, five days before the fen's own letter of the fourteenth of last month.** All four are corrected on the review pass below. **A Volume 03 writer must not carry this mislabel; the day table's band column is the one place in that prompt that is wrong, and its intervals are right.**

**And one stated deviation, with its reason, so it is not discovered later as an error.** The prompt lists three weekday statements a reviewer may check the chain against, and the third is *the far end refuses on a Tuesday, day 143*. **In the manuscript the refusal is spoken at the thorn fence on day 142, the twenty-second, a Monday, and it reaches this holding on day 143, the twenty-third, a Tuesday, when Coll Renner comes up the fen road and reports it.** The chapter's day table is exactly the prompt's (142–143, Monday–Tuesday) and every internal interval is checkable, but the refusal itself falls a day before the prompt's line. **It was left where it is** because the only way to move the fence scene to the twenty-third is to move Renner's report to the twenty-fourth, and the twenty-fourth is the morning Chapter 96 drafts the arrangement; the cascade would have cost the resolution chapter its day. **A later chapter may not say the refusal was given on the twenty-third, and may not say the fen knew of it before the twenty-third.**

## The Batch 0005 review pass — Volume 02, Chapters 89–98, and what it changed

**A review ran over the ten chapters, the four state files and the volume-close prompt after the verification pass above had been applied, and six defects were applied in place. Four of the six are one defect. No scene was replaced, no plot moved, no beat cut, no number changed except the printer's page count, and no character altered. The close-up is `reviews/volume-02-batch-0005.findings.md`.**

**1. BLOCKING — the volume's closing speech dated an answer five days before the question.** `chapter-0098.md:59` read *on the fourteenth of last month this holding told a district office in writing … and the answer came on the ninth of last month*. Day 104 is the fourteenth of last month and day 99 is the ninth of last month, so **the district answered the fen on the ninety-ninth a question put on the hundred and fourth.** This is the volume's last speech and the paragraph the whole book builds to. Now: *the answer came on the ninth of this month*, and the class of use and the rate are **split** — *The answer was a class of use, and the rate on it came on the tenth* — because Ch 89 has the schedule sent under the third point of the letter of the ninth and the cart with the four sheets arriving on the tenth, and the old sentence assigned the class itself to the tenth and contradicted the sentence before it.

**2. BLOCKING — the root cause: day 129 was moved into the wrong month, in four chapters, by the verification pass.** The month begins on day 121, so **day 129 is the ninth of *this* month and day 99 is the ninth of last month.** The pass's own governing rule says so — *inside a scene on days 121–149, days 121–150 are this month* — and then applied the opposite band at Chs 91:23, 94:27, 96:21 and 98:59, because the batch prompt's day table says *the ninth of last month (129)* in a row where the interval is right and the band is wrong. **Three chapters already said *this month* for the same event and they were right:** Ch 89:13 *the letter of the ninth of this month*, Ch 90:27 *Since the ninth it is a class of use*, Ch 93:61 *Since the ninth of this month*. All four are now **the ninth of this month**, and the prompt is annotated at its head so the label cannot travel into Volume 03.

**3. BLOCKING — the wrong band was recorded as canon in three state locations and would have propagated into the volume close and Volume 03.** Corrected in this file, in `state/current.md` and in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, and the two stale rows in the table above are struck rather than deleted, so the record of what the first pass did is still visible.

**4. BLOCKING — a figure in the same paragraph as finding 1.** *a printer in Fennmere who has set four hundred pages of our book.* The book is **two hundred pages** in Chs 60 (twice), 61, 73, 74 (twice), 76 (twice), 77, 80 and 82, and **no second printing of the book appears anywhere in the prose**; four hundred copies belongs to the Ashcombe table at Auberron and a hundred and forty is the map's run. Corrected to **two hundred pages**.

**5. NON-BLOCKING — October's offset was wrong in two files and right in the third, and the review asked for the wrong one to be changed.** The three files disagreed: the volume-close prompt said **four months back** by day 130, and the batch prompt and this file both said **five**. **Four is correct.** Days 1–30 are October, 31–60 one month on, 61–90 two, 91–120 three, 121–150 four; day 130 sits in the fifth month and October is the first, so October is four months back. This file's own month rule says so, and item 2 of the prompt-mislabel list above had already worked it out for day 4. **The volume-close prompt was therefore left at four and annotated, and the batch prompt and this file were corrected from five to four.** Ch 89's *the fourth of a month four months back* was right all along and is now backed by a reason instead of standing alone.

**6. NON-BLOCKING — six British spellings removed against a standing rule.** `bible/terminology.md` decided US spelling at the Volume 01 close and forbids reintroducing British forms. Volume 01 is clean across 166,022 words; Volume 02 had **six**: *favour* ×4 (Ch 57:109, Ch 86:17 twice, Ch 86:43), *defence* (Ch 92:31) and *travelled* (Ch 87:87). All six are now US. **A wider sweep for thirty further British forms across all forty-nine chapters returns nothing.** The volume-close prompt asked the next phase to *decide once* on spelling; the bible already decided, so the instruction now names the six sites and tells the close phase to decide nothing.

**Also recorded so the close pass does not re-open it.** The **em dash at `chapter-0092.md:27`** is the only one in the batch and **fifty-five chapters across both volumes use it**, so it is this manuscript's idiom and not an intruder. Do not flag it and do not strip dashes across the volume on the strength of a single instance.

**And the review's own word-count claims did not survive checking, and the file now carries the recomputed figures.** The batch is **28,783** words by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their headings, **range 2,620–3,103**, inside 27,000–30,000 and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter; Ch 98 is **seventeen** words under the ceiling. The figures previously recorded here and in four other files were 28,788 and 2,619–3,108; **the low end was wrong by one before this pass as well.** Volume 02 is therefore **141,818 words — 31,337 + 25,388 + 23,108 + 33,202 + 28,783** — and Volume 01's 166,022 stands. Every one of these figures has been recomputed rather than adjusted.

## New locks from Batch 0005 (volume-closing)

- Toll paid entered in two books as toll; operator standing fact in two books; last unserveable thing lost.
- Refusal clause fen's own on own door Crown cannot amend waive tax; proved by one use not argument; Crown terms contain no refusal and none defeats.
- Arrangement not charter date condition right to leave; every party walks day's notice; unenforceable except hoe; survives as commons public ledgers copyable refusal used once.
- Sixty pounds ten stands not paid not remitted not reduced file open reservation stands.
- Third condition still unnamed unguessed unwritten-for; test date gone.
- Seed-route bridge and nothing above; no regional caretaker anchor seedheart archive draw rootlight.
- No romance nothing tender; ownership not settled neither concedes.
- Nia cart twice not stopped nobody asks nobody writes why; letter sealed drawer unopened undiscussed six batches ~hundred days.
- Sera not asked Brinewake not in batch fourth refusal unnumbered; first licensed witness Renner still not Sera.
- Pulse thirteenth logged cause not established nothing under; leak five-eighths seventh hour unmended unmystery; 399 fourth month not discharged; schedule never produced layer not entitled; Thornwild unnamed long line untouched seat absent; bypass unbuilt men not back; collie uninterrupted weir not returned low map unopened; Lissa unforgiven unthanked reader anybody who comes; Wenna thirty-nine cannot read never signed name off everything only refuser; readable position not virtue; intervals checkable or not said eleven weeks only day 52.
- Ending untouched no new final enemy.
- **The levy is on sixteen households of this holding and not on forty.** Corrected in Ch 90 on the verification pass; the forty households of Ch 80 are a hundredweight of compost and stand.
- **Tova Reed has been a steward eleven years, not eleven months.** Corrected in Ch 90; *eleven months* is the season and may never be used of a tenure.
- **The reading rule is the twentieth of a month two months back, day 80.** Two references said the fourteenth and one said the twenty-second; all three now say the twentieth.
- **The fen's letter claiming to be a holder of a line is day 104, the fourteenth of last month; the district's four points are day 129, the ninth of *this* month; the terms and the rate are day 130, the tenth.** **The earlier line in this file said day 129 was the ninth of *last* month. It is not, and it was not: the month begins on day 121, so 129 − 30 = 99, which is the ninth of last month, and the district cannot answer a letter on the ninety-ninth a question put on the hundred and fourth. Corrected in Chs 91, 94, 96 and 98 by the review pass, and the batch prompt is the source of the error.** Chapter 98's closing speech now carries all three, in the right order, and the map's run of a hundred and forty.
- **October is four months back by day 130, not five.** Corrected in this file and in the batch prompt by the review pass. Days 1–30 are October, 31–60 one month after, 61–90 two, 91–120 three, 121–150 four; day 130 sits in the fifth month and October is the first. **The volume-close prompt was already right and has been left at four; this file and the batch prompt both said five and were wrong.** Chapter 89's *the fourth of a month four months back* was always right.
- **The book is two hundred pages.** Chapter 98's closing speech said a printer had set four hundred pages of it; the book is two hundred pages in Chs 60, 61, 73, 74, 76, 77, 80 and 82 and there is no second printing in the prose. **Four hundred copies belongs to the Ashcombe table at Auberron and a hundred and forty is the map's run.** Corrected in the review pass.
- **The refusal was spoken on day 142 and reached the fen on day 143.** A later chapter may not date the giving to the twenty-third or the knowing before it.

## Proposed canon changes — Batch 0005

Two required by prompt, both local instruments not world rules: (1) **local arrangement between bodies with no standing** — date condition exit unenforceable except hoe; (2) **refusal clause with no object** — untestable binds writer copyable. Neither changes power relationship ending. Recorded not silently applied.

# VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 99–108, MOVEMENT 1

**Volume 03, *The Memory Orchard*. Ten chapters, Movement 1, *Last Season's Crop*, days 151–170. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`. Its governing prompt is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`. Volume 01 is closed and audited twice. Volume 02 is closed; its close is `reviews/volume-02.findings.md` and it was run one phase later than it was planned for, because the phase that was to run it was marked done without producing it. Nothing below reopens either volume.**

## The Volume 03 dating rule, which adds one thing to Volume 02's

Months run thirty days. Days 1–30 are the first month, 31–60 the second, 61–90 the third, 91–120 the fourth, 121–150 the fifth, **151–180 the sixth.** **October is the first month and is named nowhere in Chapters 99–108.** From a scene inside days 151–170:

| Days | How a past day is named in this batch |
|---|---|
| 1–30 | *a month five months back* |
| 31–60 | *a month four months back* |
| 61–90 | *a month three months back* |
| 91–120 | *a month two months back* or *last month* |
| 121–150 | *last month* |
| 151–170 | *of this month*, *the first*, *the second*, or the bare ordinal |

**October is five months back by day 151 and is named nowhere.** Day 150 was the thirtieth, the last day of the sowing, and it is behind this batch. Day 171 and after belong to Batch 0002.

**The four things the Volume 02 Batch 0005 prompt got wrong were checked against this rule before a word was written and none of them was inherited.** Day 129 is the ninth of *last* month and day 149 is the twenty-ninth of *last* month. The band for a scene on days 121–150 is 121–150 = *this month*; the error that cost Volume 02 four chapters was reading a prompt day table instead of the governing rule, and this batch's table was checked line by line.

## Volume 03 Batch 0001 day clock, confirmed against the drafted chapters

Ch 99 days 151–152 (**Wed the first, Thu the second**) · Ch 100 days 153–154 (Fri the third, Sat the fourth) · Ch 101 day 155 (**Sun the fifth**) · Ch 102 days 156–157 (Mon the sixth, Tue the seventh) · Ch 103 days 158–159 (Wed the eighth, Thu the ninth) · Ch 104 days 160–161 (Fri the tenth, Sat the eleventh) · Ch 105 day 162 (**Sun the twelfth**) · Ch 106 days 163–164 (**Mon the thirteenth, Tue the fourteenth**) · Ch 107 days 164–165 (Tue the fourteenth, Wed the fifteenth) · Ch 108 days 166–170 (**Thu the sixteenth through Mon the twentieth**).

**The batch runs days 151–170 with no gap. Day 150 was the thirtieth of last month and a Tuesday, which follows from day 149 being a Monday, Volume 02's last day. Day 151 is the first of this month and a Wednesday, and the weekday follows from day 61 = the first = a Thursday, locked in Volume 02 Batch 0001. The three checkable weekday statements on the page are: the compost line entered on the first of the month (Wednesday), the party leaving Lantern Reach on the sixth (Monday), and the middle reading of the well house door on the sixteenth (Thursday).**

**One chapter reaches one day past its own range, deliberately, and it is Ch 106.** Its second day is the fourteenth, and its fourteenth-hour material is the seed-route ledger entries made that afternoon — the well, the arm, the standing rule. Ch 107 opens on the same fourteenth, in its **afternoon**, when a form comes up the plateau road. **Day 164 therefore belongs to the close of Ch 106 and the head of Ch 107 and must not be counted twice in any reconciliation.** No beat of one is repeated in the other. This is the Volume 02 Batch 0004 Ch 87 / Ch 88 pattern and it is the second time this manuscript has used it.

**Every relative-date expression in the batch was extracted and recomputed against the table above. Twenty-five expressions. Twenty-two are correct. The three that point forward are correct on inspection and are forward references:** Ch 105's *the thirteenth of this month*, announcing the next day's draw; Ch 106's *the fourteenth of this month*, the day the entries are made on; and Ch 108's *the sixteenth of this month* and *the twentieth*, the two later readings of the well. **One chronology defect was found and repaired on the drafting pass:** Ch 106 originally carried the well's second and third readings, on the sixteenth and the twentieth, from a chapter dated the thirteenth and the fourteenth. Those two readings were moved into Ch 108, which is the chapter that spans them, and Ch 106 now carries a line saying they are entered later in the volume and were neither moved up nor left out.

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 99–108

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| **New writing, the road** | six clauses, one page, shorter than the five it replaces; route as scheduled, not beyond the second break; rate fourpence on thirty honored as written; measured capacity entered every month under the declared, in the same hand; either end may stop on a day's notice, no reason, no appeal; copy at this end to anybody who asks; the far end's copy goes on the post when a person goes and not before; **the record is one record, copies to Cray and to Cauldron Reach, and no person holding any part of it keeps a private copy of any part of it**; term to the last day of the sowing again | Ch 99 |
| **The fifth clause's vote** | four to three, in daylight, book shut; against: the man of about fifty — *afraid of the man, not of the one place*; for: Anneke Roos, *a private copy is a thing a person can hold at a kitchen table with nobody else in the room*. Clause five read twice at the door because a man asked for it | Ch 99 |
| **Merrid Ollvar** | about fifty-four, of Cauldron Reach, Glassward high country; four houses; eleven acres; a hundred and forty-one trees; thirty-one years on the shelf; her father's name on the wall of the third house and he is the second one and is in no record; walked nine days down with a crate; **not a member of the cooperative, not a villain, not redeemed**; a buyer has offered a price the hundredweight the same four seasons and has never asked why the fruit is odd, **and has not been told**; his paper warrants the weather and not the ground; **the day on it is the seventh of next month, day 187** — see the fix pass below | Ch 100, 103, 104 |
| **The crate** | thirty-one pears, sound, stalks on, **picked on the sixteenth of a month three months back (day 76), which is eleven weeks exactly before the gate on the third (day 153)**, from a row that had not fruited since the same month last year; eleven weeks in a stone room on boards; one cut open in the yard and sound; **the sum at that price is more than this holding has taken in since the charter was made, and it is her money and not ours** | Ch 100 |
| **The reading of the fifth** | a pear root collar, second tree in the row, Sunday, no charge for the day, the test written down before the case was opened, two rules agreeing; **eleven seconds counted aloud**; four inches of rootmark hot, hand useless from the wrist down; the instrument's three lines — the collar carries a season's set in four bands and the bands are true; the set is not the same every year and the difference is in the wood and not in the root; **there is no reading here of what the tree was told, there is a reading of what it did**; **the panel is spent here and nowhere else in this batch and is quoted in no document anywhere in the book** | Ch 101 |
| **The mechanism** | the tree is not remembering, it is keeping order; a graft carries the thing put in and loses the thing that was there; a tree does the work it was told to do in the order it was told to do it; the question is what it was told, by whom, and whether the telling is still going on. **The word memory is put off the table by Tova Reed before any form can carry it** | Ch 101 |
| **The use log** | **thirteen lines**, a contact and not a reading; records the instrument, date, hour, licensed man present, and the purpose in the fen's own words; **it records contacts and never readings** | Ch 101 |
| **The party** | Marek Vale, Tova Reed, Lissa Vale, Coll Renner. **Gael Senn stays and keeps the books**, and the fen entered that no vote was taken and why, and that the reason has a person in it. Nine days up, four down, one change of horses, board at both ends refused by them and taken by her | Ch 102 |
| **The going's cost** | eleven shillings a day of horse and man; **thirteen weeks of horse at four shillings a hundredweight, a hundred and four shillings, entered in the compost line and not taken out**, and the shortfall is not three hundred and ninety-nine hundredweight this month and is not going to be next month; the last day of a month cannot be said in advance by a person who is going up a road, and it was not | Ch 102 |
| **The fieldbook's removal** | leaves the press and the building for the first time since it came into it; **the sub-office at Fennmere is told in person, at the counter, by Lissa Vale, standing there while Perrin Osse wrote it into a register and read it back**; the cost of the telling entered as a morning and half a mile of somebody's day; **Marek Vale does not ask her for it, and the entry carries her name and not his** | Ch 102, 108 |
| **The bay boards** | *day minus sixty-six*. **Eighty-five on the first, ninety on the sixth when the party goes, one hundred and four on the twentieth.** Ilsa Kern cuts them and says the number out loud, and on the twentieth she says it to an empty yard and tells nobody, and the telling was not hers to do | Ch 99, 102, 108 |
| **The March's tin** | ***inherited unmoved and put on no page in this batch*** — the word *tin* does not appear in Chapters 99–108 and nothing in the batch refers to it. The rule is unbroken and is *day minus nineteen*: **a hundred and thirty-two days at risk on the first (day 151) and a hundred and fifty-one on the twentieth (day 170)**, on a bed at **three and a half degrees**, with **both undertakings standing** — the seed house's that it will not be moved, the trust's that it will not be asked. **The batch owed the tin nothing but did not break it, and a Batch 0002 writer who wants the figure has it here and may put it on the page without inventing it.** Note that the four party were four hundred miles off the press for the whole of the range, so its not appearing is a consequence of where the batch is and not an omission anybody made | Ch 99–108 |
| **The third row at Cauldron Reach** | a graft on every tree, put in long before Merrid Ollvar came; this ground's extension **four-tenths of an inch in a year**; **the row carries eleven cuts and nine of them were dated**; the healed growth over the first cut **a shade under four tenths**, which over four tenths is a shade under one season — **and the cuts are one season old. This season.** Four more on the next row, older, not read, and nothing said about them. **Corrected on the Batch 0001 fix pass: the measurement used to be two and a quarter inches, which divides to between five and six and therefore contradicted the answer given in the same breath** | Ch 103 |
| **The pressed rootwood** | bales rotted to a gray pulp against the north wall of the yard, higher than a man, going round two sides, nobody has ever opened it, nobody at four houses has ever been short of firewood, and it is not building stone. **Lissa Vale will not open it**, because a stack is a record and a body that opens a record it is frightened of is a body that will say so later and not sooner. The family has never burned any of it and would not say why and did not have to | Ch 103 |
| **The eleven cuts** | **taken, dressed, closed, left alone long enough to close, taken again** — an order of operations, not a scar; **eleven on one row, of which nine were dated in Ch 103 and nine were cleaned and read in Ch 104, and the two that were not read are still on the row and were not guessed at**; one week, this season, at intervals a man can count; a smooth face with **a bevel on one side only and the same side on all nine read**; the growth below the cut greater than the growth above it; **no chisel and no draw-knife on the district's hire sheet at the change of horses or on the district's own sheet at the foot of the plateau**; Renner has seen that bevel once, would rather not say whose in a barn, and was not asked again | Ch 104 |
| **Two readings of the same eleven** | **Marek: a man in a hurry against a season. Tova: a list, made before he started** — a bad cut has a reason in the man's head, and a man who dresses a wound eleven times and comes back on the sixth day is not a man in a hurry. **Neither concedes. The fen's book cannot hold two findings under one line and entered none.** He says aloud that he would rather it was the hurry, and that wanting it is a man preferring the answer that lets him off | Ch 104 |
| **The third link** | **one was made in the spring and died in the spring and is not a link and is not counted; two is counted, and it is the collar of the second tree in the row, which is the tree in the pot, read on the fifth.** Links are numbered: one is the dead one, two is the collar, three is attempted on the twelfth, four fails — **so two and three are the whole of what there is and the fen is two links down on the plateau after Ch 105**, and the number does not go up; a scion of the second tree set into the limb of the third a foot below the face; **the number does not go up and a fourth is not attempted because it fails**; the scion's own extension two and a half inches on the day, four-tenths the day after, three-tenths the second morning — **a union holding at a quarter of what it should, and the row will hold that tree at about three-quarters and not the whole**; the face is not cut into and the scion is not to be read with an instrument this season | Ch 105 |
| **The scion** | out of the second tree, which is the tree that fruited eleven weeks out of season; **in four years nobody will be able to tell which is which from the ground**; entered in advance of the thing happening, which is the first time this fen has done that | Ch 105 |
| **Stage 3, first use** | **the thirteenth, from about the second hour until about the sixth, in the lee of a north wall at Cauldron Reach, on a living path through a scion three-tenths of an inch a day, with a licensed man holding a lamp and a steward holding a page.** Water, heat and rootlight together; four feet; four hours; **he can move flow through a living path of his own making and cannot do it at range, without a living path, or twice in a week. This is not a promotion. It is a thing four houses drink from.** | Ch 106 |
| **The first severe rootburn** | heat past the four inches of the rootmark and into the elbow, inside and outside, **and it is not the mark and the mark is where it was**; fever inside two hours, none on the fourteenth, the eighteenth or the twentieth; **three fingers of the left hand gone from the second joint, restored on the second morning**; **taste gone entire with the fever, and sour not back by the twentieth and no promise**; **an hour at about the fifth that is not there and that he does not intend to find out**; he could not stop it by wanting to stop it; at the sixth hour it stopped because a licensed man said *it is a pear tree and you are a man, and one of us has to be the one who is wrong about which* | Ch 106, 108 |
| **The well at Cauldron Reach** | cut in pale rootwood, stone windlass, bucket and rope, **her rule is a nail mark read at the fourth hour on the sixteenth of every month, and the fourteenth of this month was an extra reading because Marek Vale asked her for it in front of witnesses**; **the third nail since the first of a month two months back (day 91)**; **nine feet on the sixteenth of last month (day 136), five feet and a half at the fourth hour of the fourteenth (day 164), five feet and five inches on the sixteenth (day 166), five feet and four inches on the twentieth (day 170)**; no rain worth the name in nine days; no other water within a mile that four houses use; **the shortfall is entered against the draw and not against the weather, the season or the fault of the ground, and there is no entry in this volume for the well coming back and the fen will not be predicting one** | Ch 105, 106, 108 |
| **The standing rule about the engineer** | **the first rule this body has written about one of its own people instead of about the ground**: nothing is to be attempted again by the engineer of record until a person has asked him in a room, on a day, what is the matter with his arm, and he has said so in a room, on a day, and a licensed man has written it down. **No number of days is entered, because a number of days would be a promise.** Proposed by the man of about seventy at a reading in a yard, not by the steward | Ch 106 |
| **The inventory, fourth part** | a schedule of records held; a list that is not a copy; part one a work and its result, part two a work and its reading, **part three a work, its reading, and a direction given to another person**; **part three is held under a standing condition that it is unfit for evidentiary use — it exists and does not count, may not be produced in any proceeding, letter, inventory return or dispute, and no officer of the district may remove the condition, including the officer who signs the letters**; **a holder who cannot classify a record states so, and the statement is itself a classification**; six items, four digits at the head, six at the foot, a box for a district holding number | Ch 107 |
| **The definition that decides the volume** | *a direction given to another person is a record in which somebody is told what to do by somebody who was not the one who did the work. It is not a reading. It is not a result. It is an order.* Tova: **then it is the order that matters, and not the work.** The man of about fifty: *somebody wrote down what to do, and somebody else was told, and we have spent eleven years learning that a readable thing gets indexed and taxed and we did not once think to ask whether it gets obeyed* | Ch 107 |
| **Sabin Roell** | records officer at the district office at the foot of the plateau, about thirty-four; came up with the cart on the fifteenth and gave her name once; **three things said in the order she says them, including that she looked twice on purpose for a power she expected to find and did not find**; expected six returns and got four refusals in four years, two of the other three in a longer sentence and worse reasons, and said this one is on a line of its own and that is not a small thing to hand back; **not a member of the cooperative and not a villain and not made one** | Ch 107 |
| **The free offer** | the district will hold copies of anything a holder places with it, at the office at the foot of the plateau, on the district's storage, in perpetuity, **at no charge**, for safekeeping only; safekeeping is not a classification and places nothing in any part; the holder may produce a copy on application, **and the office will not produce it and no officer may be asked for it**. **Declined in writing in one line**, with the plain reason entered — a copy at the district's storage is a copy the district can declare part three — and the good reason left out | Ch 107 |
| **Tova's paper** | she says she has read **the shape of a free offer with a signature attached** before, is not going to take it, and is not going to say where she read it. **Nobody asked her. The fen entered that the question was available to anybody in that yard and was taken by nobody, that it is available again tomorrow, and that the fen has entered that it will not be taken.** This is the Volume 01 Ch 4 plant paying off. **It has no number, no date, no place, no name and no source in the prose and none may be given to it** | Ch 107 |
| **The reading of the fieldbook** | the second of the afternoon of the seventeenth, at Cauldron Reach, on a hired trestle, in a stone barn, with a licensed reader, two witnesses, a steward, an engineer and about nine other people. **Page the first:** a column head and *confirmed — set — 4*, and above it in the same ink **Aldren's last line of figures — *Seventeen. Set in the low. Eleven. Watch the fourth beat in the small hours.*** **Page the second:** an impossible year, and beside it in a later hand *probably a copying error; to be treated as one, and no inference to be drawn from it* — **and the later hand is Lissa Vale's, and she has never told anybody she wrote it.** **Page the third, the last page, a work order:** district paper of a shape not used here for a long time, a sheet of three, **an office named at the head and not explained anywhere in this holding's book**, a direction to a named node on a named day in a named week of a year that is eight years ago and is the year the shaft was, and at the foot under a printed head for a signature **a hand that makes the letter *W* the way it made the *W* in *weir* and the way it made the *W* in *wire*** — the same identification made in Volume 02 on a register of authorisations and never asked about | Ch 108 |
| **The work order** | *Third week, Thursday. Take the branch at the upper collar, the whole of it, and dress. Flow at the head is not what the board says and the board is not mine. **Necessary.** Do not enter this sheet against the node. Enter it against me.* **A record suggesting his father chose to cut a branch during a real emergency. No reason on the page. Nobody in that barn sees a vision and nobody speaks to a dead man** | Ch 108 |
| **Four copies** | Cray on the ordinary post in an envelope with no number; Perrin Osse at the sub-office down the fen road, with a covering line naming the office at the head of the form and asking nothing; **one in Merrid Ollvar's own hand, signed at the foot with her name and the date and nothing else, and the fen entered on the same line of the same book that she has never signed a piece of paper in her life and that she signed this one**; and **one that is not the fen's** — inside Coll Renner's coat, down the plateau road on the eighteenth, **a licensed man carrying a copy of a reading in his own name with his own license on it, which no office can call a record of a holder because it is not one.** **The seventh column is copied as a line: head, rows, and the ruled blank underneath** | Ch 108 |
| **The fieldbook's standing** | closed at about the fourth hour of the eighteenth, in its own box, in a room with a lock at the first house on that shelf; **not going back down the road**; **may not be opened again in this season — not by Lissa Vale, not by a licensed man, not by an officer of any district, and not by a person who comes up that road and says they are from an office.** Her first reason is the arguable one (a book open twice in one season is a book somebody can say was altered between the openings, and the only copy in the world is a copy of a reading and not of a book) and her second is entered because it is the true one (she did not open it once in nine years because she was afraid of it, and has now opened it **once**, this afternoon, and says she is not opening it again). **Corrected on the fix pass: the chapter said she had opened it twice in three days and nothing on the page supports a second opening.** The book was last out of the press on **the twenty-eighth of a month two months back (day 118)**, when it was put on a chair in a seed house and a floor was read out of the middle of the room, **and it was not opened then either** — forty-nine days before the seventeenth, and it has not been out of the press since. **The month before the flood is wrong and is gone: the flood is day 47** | Ch 108 |
| **The batch's finding, in one line** | **eleven cuts and a word and a shelf** — asked for by the man of about seventy and entered whole, and it fits on a line and there is no more of it than that | Ch 108 |

## Section-nine notes: twenty-second to twenty-sixth

Twenty-one at the end of Volume 02 → **twenty-second** Ch 99 (the second), **twenty-third** Ch 102 (the seventh), **twenty-fourth** Ch 104 (the eleventh), **twenty-fifth** Ch 107 (the fifteenth), **twenty-sixth** Ch 108 (the twentieth). 22 argument carries sentence; 23 argument carries sentence; 24 **argument** carries sentence; 25 **record and the sentence is off** with the reason written underneath — the note is the record of a refusal and a refusal is not an argument; 26 **argument** carries sentence (Lissa Vale's decision on the fieldbook). **Stands on twenty-five of twenty-six since Volume 01, and on four of five in this batch; the one off is off with a reason and the reason is on the page.**

**The volume continues the standing joke and it is not a joke: a body with no charter keeps writing notes under section nine of a charter that lapsed at midnight on the twenty-ninth of last month, and every one of the five says so on its face.** There is no charter. There is no motion, no form and no officer. **The note is evidence of what this body intended and not of what it did, and that mechanism is the only reason any of this can be said in a room and written down afterwards.**

## Fieldbook panel: one spent, in Chapter 101

**Spent in Ch 101, by Coll Renner, with his own clip, on the root collar of a pear, in a yard at Lantern Reach on a Sunday.** Three plain lines and the instrument's own account of itself. **Not a finding, not a verdict, and not quoted as authority by any character in any document anywhere in the book.** The panel's evident place in Ch 107 at the inventory would have been a trap — the instrument does not know what a part three is. **Volume 03 allowance is one per batch: Batch 0001 spent, and Batches 0002–0005 have one each available.**

**The fieldbook itself was opened for the first time in a year in Ch 108, and there is still no use log for it. There has never been one and none may be supplied, proposed, or thanked for. The use log is thirteen lines and records contacts and never readings. The whole room knew and not one of them said a word.**

## Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0001

**Marek Vale is Stage 3, Drawwright, at the end of this batch, and the stage arrived by use in Ch 106 and is paid for in the same chapter and is not recovered in it.**

- **Stage 2 remains true and is entered in Ch 101 as Stage 2 and as nothing more:** a small reliable graft between a living plant and a limited local branch, the anchor and the practitioner both recording it, the mark visible and permanent.
- **The rootmark is unchanged: four inches along the inside of the left forearm, branching twice, the color of a healed burn and not a bruise, from Ch 57 of Volume 02.** The burn in Ch 106 runs past it and **is not a mark**. **A second rootmark may not appear and the mark may not be lengthened.**
- **Two links, three at the outside, a fourth fails and is not attempted and is named as a thing that will not be attempted.** After Ch 105 the fen is two links down.
- **A draw is water, heat and rootlight together, through a living path, over about four feet, for about four hours.** No draw at range. No draw without a living path. No second draw inside a week. **No return by rootlight at range, no bead, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive, no memory read.** The scarroot sequence is read by **counting healed cuts and dating them with a rule**, which is engineering, and no vision appears anywhere in this batch and nobody speaks to a dead man.
- **The standing rule of Ch 106 binds him for the rest of the volume** until a person asks him in a room, on a day, about his arm and a licensed man writes it down.
- **Volume 02's no-draw rule is spent and may not be reapplied.** The **Continuity Office has not been named on the page**; an unnamed office is named at the head of a column on a work order form and is not explained.

## New locks from Volume 03 Batch 0001

- **Stage 3 is spent and paid.** Rootburn with darkened veins past the rootmark into the elbow, fever, three fingers, taste gone and sour not back by the twentieth, and an unaccounted hour he does not intend to find out. **The cost is on other people's ground: a well on a plateau three and a half feet down on a household bucket, entered against the man and not the weather, and not refunded in this volume.**
- **The rootmark is not lengthened and no second mark appears.**
- **The first rule this body has written about one of its own people, not about the ground, and it is in force.**
- **Eleven cuts, one row, one week, this season, dressed by a man who did not come back; a bevel no tool in the district owns; and two readings of it, one of which is not resolved and is not going to be in this volume.**
- **A work order in Aldren Vale's hand, dated the year of the shaft, saying *necessary*, giving no reason, and instructing a clerk not to enter it against the node.** Nobody in that barn saw a vision. **Marek cannot find out whether his father was right and has entered that he is not going to be able to.**
- **The fieldbook is closed for the season and may not be opened again. It is in a locked room at the first house on the plateau, not at Lantern Reach, and the fen does not get it back because it is theirs.**
- **Four copies of the reading exist and one of them is not the fen's.** The seventh column is copied as a line including the blank.
- **The part three category exists and takes a record away by listing it, and no officer of any district may remove the condition.** The volume's antagonist is a form and an office and it is correct throughout.
- **Tova's paper is spent as a shape and given no number, no date, no place, no name and no source.** Nobody asked and the fen entered that it will not be asked.
- **The new writing's fifth clause stands: no person holding any part of the record keeps a private copy of any part of it**, carried 4–3 against the man of about fifty's vote, and the fen is a body that cannot get its record back because it gave a copy away.
- **The middle reading of the well house door happened in an empty yard and the count went to twenty-two, and the two ends of the body could not check each other.** The rule is a decision and not a mood.
- **The fourteenth beat is logged, cause not established, licensed person present none, nothing written underneath it, and nobody on the plateau knows for a week.** The leak is at Lantern Reach and is not mended and was not measured in this batch. **Its position on the twenty-first of this month, day 171, for the next writer, and it is a day number and not a name because a name moves at the month turn: the last entry in the book about it is the seventh hour of day 138, which is the eighteenth of last month in Chapters 109–113 and a month two months back in Chapters 114–118, and the two before that are day 135 and day 121. So the figure in the book is thirty-three days old when Batch 0002 opens, and it is five-eighths, and it is not mended, and it is not a mystery.** Gael Senn is the one who measures it, at the seventh hour on most mornings, and **she did not go up the road and is not the reason there is a gap: eighteen days of the gap are days the whole fen was in that yard, and the gap cannot be dated because a practice that was never written down leaves no account of itself.** An earlier draft of the Batch 0002 prompt said the gap was fourteen days and put the cause on the party going up; both were wrong and the prompt now carries the day number instead.
- **Merrid Ollvar and Sabin Roell are new named people and neither is a member of anything. No new named member of the cooperative was created.** The crew is unchanged: Ilsa Kern, Jonas Byre, Anneke Roos, Gael Senn, Lissa Vale, Dunmar Fitch, Marek Vale, plus Tova Reed and Coll Renner and the man of about fifty and the man of about seventy, who remain held by function.
- **The 399 hundredweight has entered its fifth month and the going has made the shortfall worse, and the true figure for next month is not 399 and this holding does not know what it is.**
- **The board on the door is in its ninth month of service and the count of the readings of the seventh column is twenty-two.**
- **Nia's cart is past the gate twice in a season and has not stopped and nobody has asked why. Her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across six batches and about a hundred and twenty days of season.** It was not touched in this batch and nothing in this batch refers to it.
- **Sera Quill is not in this batch, is not asked about Brinewake, and her fourth refusal is unnumbered, unasked and may not be numbered.** She is implied twice and only twice: by a name nobody says out loud on the rider's letterhead, and by somebody asking who else could read a book and getting a name and not saying it aloud. **Nothing more.**
- **The Thornwild settlement is not named, the long line is untouched, and the word *seat* does not appear. The bypass is unbuilt and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. The works schedule behind the one certified page is not produced and the applicant is not entitled to it.**
- **No romance. Nothing tender on the page.** The milestone is that they choose not to keep private records, and it is argued as a clause in a fen document and as a refusal in a yard, and it is not a scene between two people.
- **The seven shapes from Ch 98 are not a map, not a printer and not a reason, and no character in this batch treats them as one.**
- **Ending untouched. No new final enemy.** Rung 4 (the Pale Orchard) has not entered and may not enter before Volume 04. Rung 6 (Iona Vey) is not named.

## The Batch 0001 fix pass — what a later writer may not undo

**The review phase behind `reviews/volume-03-batch-0001.findings.md` was the `next-0003` phase, and its log was cut off before it wrote a report**; its mechanical pass was re-run and completed by hand, and the findings are that file — **twenty-nine, eleven blocking and eighteen should-fix**, all applied in place, **no chapter restarted and no planned plot moved.** **The log is not named here and is not named there, because `logs/` is gitignored and no log file is in any commit: the earlier citation of `logs/next-0003.review.log` pointed at a file no reader can open, and the checkable provenance is the phase and the state its work stopped in.** The batch is 28,570 words, range 2,620–3,119, as that pass left it; **a later pass has since moved it to 28,582 words, range 2,620–3,119**, changing one speech and one note in Ch 104 and nothing else in the prose.

**These six are the corrections that changed a fact, and each one now contradicts something a state file said before the pass. They are settled.**

1. **The day on the buying paper is the seventh of next month, day 187.** The batch said *the eleventh* in eight places and the day-counts around it contradicted each other and the eleventh, and the fix pass moved the date to day 187, which is the first day of Ch 117 in the queued Batch 0002, whose card is *the day on the buying paper arrives*. **No chapter may re-date it.** **Corrected again on the adoption pass, and this is the third and last time it will be said: the fix pass gave day 187 the *name* the seventeenth of next month, and that name is day 197. Day 187 is the seventh of the seventh month. All ten instances in Chapters 99–108 now read the seventh of next month, every day-count on the page (thirty-five from the second, four weeks from the ninth, twenty-seven from the tenth, twenty-five from the twelfth, seventeen from the twentieth) is unchanged and still resolves to 187, and no scene moved.** The arithmetic was always right; only the name was wrong. **The name is now the seventh of next month everywhere, in the prose, in these state files, and in the queued Batch 0002 prompt.**
2. **The eleven cuts are one season old, this season.** The measurement is **a shade under four tenths** of healed growth against four tenths of annual extension. It is not two and a quarter inches, which divides to between five and six years. **The row carries eleven; nine were dated in Ch 103 and nine were cleaned and read in Ch 104, and the two that were not read were not guessed at.**
3. **The links are numbered and one is counted.** **One** is the graft in the pot at Lantern Reach that died in the spring and is not a link; **two** is the collar of the second tree in the row, which is the tree in the pot, read on the fifth; **three** is the scion attempted on the twelfth; **four** fails and is not attempted. **So two and three are the whole of what there is, the number does not go up, and the fen is two links down after Ch 105.** The Batch 0001 card's *two active links already* is the half of it that is wrong.
4. **Merrid Ollvar's pears were picked on the sixteenth of a month three months back — day 76, which is eleven weeks before the gate on the third.** Not the sixth of a month four months back, which is day 36 and sixteen weeks.
5. **The well's rule is the fourth hour on the sixteenth of every month.** The sixteenth of last month is her last ordinary reading at **nine feet**; **the fourteenth of this month was an extra reading she gave because Marek Vale asked her for it in front of witnesses**, and it is five and a half. The shortfall is three and a half feet and is entered against the draw.
6. **The fieldbook was last out of the press on the twenty-eighth of a month two months back, day 118, when it was put on a chair for a floor to be read out of the middle of a room, and it was not opened then either.** It is forty-nine days before Ch 108, not a hundred and seventy, and it is not the month before the flood — **the flood is day 47.** **It is opened once in this batch, on the seventeenth, and Lissa Vale's reason for closing it is a book open twice in one season, not twice in three days.**

**And the two standing italic lines at the foot of the section-nine notes are not duplicates to be fixed.** *An argument. This is worth nothing against a cut.* closes Chs 102, 104 and 108 here, and it closes fifteen chapters across Volumes 01 and 02; *This is a record and not an argument, and the sentence is off this note for that reason…* closes Chs 99 and 107 and closes Volume 02 Chapters 84 and 96. **They are the manuscript's formula. Kept verbatim, and the reason is recorded here so that no later close spends a finding on them or varies them to look original.**

---

## Proposed canon changes — Volume 03 Batch 0001

Three, none of which changes a world rule, a power rule, a relationship lock, or the ending; all three are local instruments and are recorded rather than silently applied.

1. **The new writing's fifth clause** — a one-record rule binding a body with no standing not to keep private copies of its own public record. It is enforceable by nobody and that is its whole worth, in the same way the refusal clause of Ch 92 was unenforceable and that was its point.
2. **The part three condition** — a standing condition on a schedule of records by which a record exists and does not count and no officer may remove the condition. A new instrument class, and the volume's antagonist is built out of it.
3. **The standing rule about the engineer of record** — the first rule in this manuscript's ninety-eight chapters that binds one of the protagonist's own people rather than the ground, and it was proposed by a man of about seventy and not by the steward. **It is the mechanism the rest of the volume runs on and it is a rule, not a speech.**

---

# VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 109–118, MOVEMENT 2

**Volume 03, *The Memory Orchard*, Movement 2, *The Thing In The Yard*. Ten chapters, days 171–190. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`. Its governing prompt is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`. Volume 01 and Volume 02 are closed. Nothing below reopens either volume and nothing below moves a date the Batch 0001 pass settled.**

## The dating rule across the month turn, which is this batch's spine

Days 1–30 the first month, 31–60 the second, 61–90 the third, 91–120 the fourth, 121–150 the fifth, **151–180 the sixth, 181–210 the seventh.** Day 171 is the twenty-first of the sixth month; **day 180 is the thirtieth and is the last reading day of that month; day 181 is the first of the seventh and the month turns there, between Ch 113 and Ch 114.** A past day is therefore named differently on the two sides of that line, and the names are:

| Day | Named, in Chs 109–113 | Named, in Chs 114–118 |
|---|---|---|
| 1–30 | *a month five months back* | *a month six months back* |
| 31–60 | *a month four months back* | *a month five months back* |
| 61–90 | *a month three months back* | *a month four months back* |
| 91–120 | *a month two months back* | *a month three months back* |
| 121–150 | *last month* | *a month two months back* |
| 151–180 | *of this month*, or the bare ordinal | *last month* |
| 181–190 | — | *of this month*, *the first*, *the second*, or the bare ordinal |

**October is the first month, is five months back by day 171, and is named nowhere.** *March* is not used as a month anywhere; the only capitalized *March* in the batch is *the March's tin*, which is the Salt March seed trust's seed and not a calendar.

## Volume 03 Batch 0002 day clock, confirmed against the drafted chapters

Ch 109 days 171–172 (**Tue the twenty-first, Wed the twenty-second**) · Ch 110 days 173–174 (Thu the twenty-third, Fri the twenty-fourth) · Ch 111 days 175–176 (Sat the twenty-fifth, Sun the twenty-sixth) · Ch 112 days 177–178 (Mon the twenty-seventh, Tue the twenty-eighth) · Ch 113 days 179–180 (**Wed the twenty-ninth, Thu the thirtieth**) · Ch 114 days 181–182 (**Fri the first, Sat the second of next month**) · Ch 115 days 183–184 (Sun the third, Mon the fourth of next month) · Ch 116 days 185–186 (Tue the fifth, Wed the sixth of next month) · Ch 117 days 187–188 (**Thu the seventh, Fri the eighth of next month**) · Ch 118 days 189–190 (Sat the ninth, Sun the tenth of next month).

**The batch runs days 171–190 with no gap and no chapter reaches past its own range.** Day 170 was the twentieth of the sixth month, a Monday, and is Volume 03 Batch 0001's last day. Day 171 is a Tuesday, which follows from day 151 being a Wednesday (Batch 0001's first day) and twenty days on. **No day is shared between two chapters in this batch and none is counted twice.**

**The four days down the plateau road are the twenty-seventh through the thirtieth.** The party of four left the shelf at about the sixth hour on the twenty-seventh and came onto the fen road at Lantern Reach on the thirtieth, four days, one change of horses, **and the going is still nine days up and four down and no chapter may shorten either.** The arrival on the thirtieth is what puts the steward in front of the well house door in Ch 113, and it is a figure on the day and not a scene.

**The three checkable weekday statements on the page are:** the first of next month, on which the examination and the compost line fall, a **Friday**; the four days down begun on the twenty-seventh, a **Monday**; and the last reading of the well house door of the sixth month, on the thirtieth, a **Thursday**. All three agree with the table.

**One geographic decision this batch had to make and a later writer must honour.** The fen is at Lantern Reach from Ch 113's second day to the end of the batch. **Nothing on the shelf after the thirtieth is narrated from inside a person standing in that yard, and the three things that had to happen up there are handled as documents and as paid messengers:** the household's decision on the day letter and the man at the second house's ninth day come down the road as a four-line message in Merrid Ollvar's hand with the man at the second house's name against three of its lines, brought by a man hired at the change of horses at four shillings a day, and the hire is entered in the compost line; the buyer's day is narrated on the shelf; and Marek Vale's unasked answer goes **up** the road on the seventh of next month and the fen enters that it will arrive about nine days behind a decision already taken, and nobody on the shelf is going to be asked to read it. **No batch may have a character appear on the shelf and back at the fen inside ten days.** The road is nine days up and four down and the manuscript's arithmetic has been audited in three volumes.

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 109–118

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| **The leak, first measured in a yard** | **five-eighths of an inch**, twice, two rules, the second of them the one that reads smaller on that corner, pump on, water into the housing and not into the pit, **the pit not up**; read at the seventh hour of the twenty-first of this month with three people in the yard; the figure said out loud **for the first time in two years** | Ch 109 |
| **The gap, and the finding** | the last entry in the book concerning it is the seventh hour of **day 138**, and the reading in Ch 109 is **thirty-three days** later; the figure in the book was thirty-three days old and had been carried forward as though it were current and **nobody in that yard decided to carry it forward**; the party left on day 157 and that is a **different interval of fourteen days** and is not the reason; **eighteen days of the gap are days the whole of the body was standing in that yard**; **the fen cannot say on which mornings it was measured and on which it was not, because a practice that was never written down leaves no account of itself, and the absence of the account is not evidence of any day in particular** | Ch 109 |
| **The practice entered** | the reading of the joint at the pump housing at the seventh hour, two rules, pump on, pit not up, **Gael Senn's name against it**, the figure entered with the hour and read back before it is initialled, **no due date and none to be invented**, proposed by the man of about seventy at a hard standing at the ninth hour and not by the steward and not by the engineer | Ch 109 |
| **The batch's hinge sentence** | *a rule that has to be remembered by the person it was made for is not a rule, it is a habit with a number in it* — said by the man of about seventy, and **given back to Marek Vale in Ch 115 and not answered** | Ch 109, 115 |
| **Jonas Byre's account** | the return main was kept open by three men who remembered it, and when one of them was not able to cross the ditch for a month nobody knew for three weeks and there was ice in the low end and about nine hundredweight of return dug out by hand; **I was the one who remembered it, and remembering it was the whole of the work**; and he is forty-eight and does not get up at the fifth hour and does not go down four feet, and asked for both facts in the same book as the figure | Ch 109 |
| **The stack** | pressed rootwood in bales against the north wall, going round two sides, higher than a man and longer than the barn, **sixty-one bales** counted aloud twice by the man at the second house; pressed hot and green so that nothing in it was ever wood and it will never be wood again; a heap of wet pulp the size of a small cart in the middle of the yard and no place on the shelf to put it; **nothing at four houses has ever burned any of it and the family would not say why and did not have to** | Ch 110 |
| **Who opened it** | **Merrid Ollvar, herself, on the twenty-third, at about the eighth hour, with a knife**, five hours, alone, and **three people asked to be allowed to do it for her and were refused three times with three different reasons** — Marek Vale on the wind and her hands, Coll Renner on the ground that a record dug into is not a record taken out, and the man at the second house on **it is my father's ground and in thirty years there will be a story in this valley about the day the valley let a stranger open the stack** | Ch 110 |
| **What was in it** | the outside a gray pulp to the depth of a hand; inside the wedge the wood sound, pale, short, **cut in lengths of about a foot and a half, every length within an inch of every other**, and **a single bevel of about an inch and a half on one edge of every cut end, the same edge on all of them** | Ch 110 |
| **Marek Vale's one sentence, and the batch's turn** | **the cutting is done and the pressing is done by two different people, or in two different trades, and a press that good is not a family press** — the same bevel, the same side, the same country, a second place in the valley, in rootwood stacked as waste, in a yard in which nobody has ever burned a bale of it | Ch 110 |
| **Lissa Vale's refusal paid off** | *A stack is a record. A body that opens a record it is frightened of is a body that will say so later and not sooner.* Carried four hundred miles and obeyed; and on the twenty-third she stands in a stranger's yard hearing about a thing she could have read, and asks for the refusal to be read twice, once for the part where she was right and once for the part where it cost her something to be | Ch 110 |
| **The dating method for the sound wood** | a rootwood seam lays down one layer a year while it is alive, the layers are countable with a rule, a lens and a lamp and go from the outside in, and **a layer on this shelf is about a twentieth of an inch, which is an eighth of what a pear limb puts on here**; the method was said out loud before it was used | Ch 111 |
| **The date of the sound wood** | the nine lengths read six, six, seven, five, six, seven, six, five, six; the core of the pile **twenty-eight layers, going back without a break, which is twenty-eight years, inside Merrid Ollvar's own thirty-one years and inside her father's time**; at four places in those twenty-eight there is a band where the layer is thicker and the wood is different, **at intervals of seven years, seven years and six years, the last of them about six years back**, with six or seven bare years between in which nothing came off that seam at all | Ch 111 |
| **The maintenance order** | a man who presses rootwood for a living does not take what he wants, he takes what he can take in the order he can take it, **outside first, then the wet, then the core**, because a core cut live bleeds on the press and the bale goes at the seam; and the order is the same every time and the order is in the wood. **Not a vision. A hand's method, dated.** | Ch 111 |
| **The graft in the waste** | **three living grafts in three bales at the core of the pile where the press did not reach, cut to cut, fitted, and bound in bast**, one living seam joined to another, put in with the waste; no more than three in sixty-one; out of the fourth band, about six years back; **no nursery within a hundred miles of that shelf**, and a family that has never signed a piece of paper and does not know what a graft is | Ch 111 |
| **Renner's finding and his limit** | *Yes, it is a graft. Somebody made this on purpose, they made it to keep, and they put it in with the waste. That is how a man hides it.* **Nobody in that yard said the word after him, and Merrid Ollvar said it — that somebody was hiding something in her yard — and was not answered**, and the fen entered that it declined to ask and that the declining is the finding. And: **a graft in a press is nobody's hobby**, but **I cannot read intent in wood**, so neither hiding nor keeping is entered | Ch 111 |
| **The road down** | going up ninety-nine shillings, nine days at eleven a day; **coming down a hundred and four**, the trestle and the bench and the case and a ledger four hundred miles each way, and the last two days of a descent being the first two days of somebody else's ascent and the fare not being the same; **and the trestle and the bench are charged to the compost line at four shillings a day and two shillings and eightpence** | Ch 112 |
| **The return of inventories, six lines** | one, the door and the six clauses, part two. Two, the seed-route ledger, part two, **and the count of readings under the seventh column decided to be a reading, by name, so that a person in four years can disagree with the decision**. Three, the three books, part two, **and there is a fourth thing in a shut drawer and it is not listed and it is not asked about and nobody asked about it**. Four, the fieldbook reading, part two, **true and incomplete** — a reading of a pear root collar with a licensed reader on a hired trestle on another district's ground, and the name is not on the one that goes to the office. Five, the licensed man's own copy, entered so that a person in four years does not read the return and think the body has hidden a thing. Six, **the work order is not listed**, because it is a page in a book that is not the fen's and a return is a list of this holder's records and not a list of what other people have written inside a thing the body was lent | Ch 112 |
| **The answer, day 185** | **held**, six times, one against each item; **no item classified, no classification requested, nothing required of the holder further than that the return has been made**; and a second line on the office's own sheet: **the word "held" is a fact about a holder and not about a record, an item entered as held is not thereby in any part, the parts are entered by the office and not by a holder, and a holder who has not stated a part has not said anything about the record at all.** Returned in seven days | Ch 116 |
| **The well, the standing figure in a yard** | read at the fourth hour of the thirtieth by the occupier, by her own hand and her own nail, said out loud before it was written down, which is not her rule and is the fen's: **five feet and two inches**; nine feet on the sixteenth of last month, five feet five inches on the sixteenth of this month, five feet and four inches on the twentieth; **shortfall three feet and ten inches**; **no rain worth the name on that shelf in twenty-five days**; **the draw's three and a half feet entered against the draw and the other half inch entered against the ground, in a different hand, in the same ledger, on the same day**; **no entry anywhere for that well coming back** | Ch 113, 116, 118 |
| **The three options and the decision** | a wet place a mile round at the foot of the slope, which is a trough in a seep and is gone by the last week of the summer; a house four hundred yards below the shelf with a well, **a debt and not a favour**; a well at the second house, older, out of use, his father's, on his ground, two men and a day. **Decided on the sixth of next month by the household alone, in daylight, the fen not consulted: four houses on a day letter, one drawing each in turn, the first house a Tuesday because there are four people in it and the pail is heavy in the heat, for a month, to be looked at again on the first of the month after, at the well, with whoever is standing there** | Ch 113, 116 |
| **The man at the second house** | **you spent a bucket**; he says he is **not angry yet** and would like it entered that he said that in those words; he gives himself until **the fifteenth of next month, day 195, which is Batch 0003's business**, on the grounds Lissa Vale gave him in a barn on the fourteenth of last month — that a man who gave a thing away in writing before he did it cannot be helped today and there is nothing in him to have yet — and says **there is now about as much in him as there is going to be**; the ninth day is in the fen's book so that a man in nine miles cannot find out afterwards that it was never said | Ch 116 |
| **The door** | read out whole on the thirtieth at the tenth hour in a yard of about twenty, by the steward, **with the reader, the clerk and twenty people in front of it, the first time in eleven years**, entered as a fact about a day and not a moment; **the count of the readings of the seventh column went to twenty-three, and to twenty-four on the first of next month in a full yard by four**, and the last time the two ends of this body stood in front of the same count in the same hour was never | Ch 113, 114 |
| **The boards** | *day minus sixty-six*: **one hundred and fourteen on the thirtieth, said out loud in a yard for the first time in eleven years** at the man of about fifty's asking, and **one hundred and twenty-four on the tenth of next month** | Ch 113, 118 |
| **The compost line, sixth month, day 181** | entered **by Gael Senn, in the steward's own book, in a hand that is not the steward's**, by an arrangement four months old that Anneke Roos asked for and the steward granted: nothing put in, none available, specification four hundred hundredweight, **shortfall four hundred and fifty-two hundredweight, ninety pounds eight at four shillings** (400, plus 26 for thirteen weeks of horse at 104 shillings, plus 26 for the descent at 104), **carried forward and not discharged, and the run of it six months and not discharged in six, and it is not to be softened in the writing**; the reader was the man of about fifty; Lissa Vale read it back and said from the first of next month it is entered in whatever hand it is in and the reader is the man of about fifty and not her, **because a book with one page in another hand is a book anybody can check and a book with every page in one hand is a book only one person can check** | Ch 114 |
| **The count of the seventh column, full history in this volume** | twenty-one on the first, twenty-two on the sixteenth in an empty yard, **twenty-three on the thirtieth in a full yard with the steward standing in front of it, twenty-four on the first of next month in a full yard by four** | Ch 113, 114 |
| **The beat** | **the fifteenth**, at the seventh hour of the twenty-first of this month, nine in the quarter hour with one run of three, at the rail, cause not established, licensed person present none, nothing written underneath it; **not logged since and the column is open and the fen has not opened it** | Ch 109, 118 |
| **The standing rule, satisfied** | on the third of next month at about the eleventh hour, in the seed house, bay doors open, fourteen people in the room, clerk, reader and licensed man present, **on a Sunday, with no form and no notice on the door, and the day named on the second by a member of this body**. Tova Reed asked it on behalf of the body and said so first. He answered: the mark does not hurt; the arm is not right above the elbow; four minutes to change hands; a pail carried on the second of this month with two changes of hand and not reported then; **and that he does not know whether he could do it, that he did not say no, and that not knowing is the whole of the answer.** The entry of the twentieth of last month stands entire, is not withdrawn and **is not reconciled** | Ch 115 |
| **The second arm entry** | from the four inches of the mark to **the top of the shoulder, which it was not on the twentieth of last month**; a place at the middle of the inside of the forearm that is not the color of the skin either side of it and did not take the color back; **it is longer than on the twentieth of last month, it is not better, it is longer, and it is written as a length and not as a decline**; recommendation unchanged: **fit for ordinary work, fit for a scion and a graft, not answering for a draw** | Ch 115 |
| **The rule's hole** | the rule is satisfied and says nothing whatever about what comes after the stop; **a rule about a stop, once the stop has been asked after and answered, is a rule about waiting**; a second draw is on a table and nothing in the rule forbids it; **Marek Vale gives the position that he is not going to use it, and his reason is not the rule and not the burn — it is that the man at the second house has not been angry with him yet, and if he goes back up that road with a thing in his hands that moves water then whatever the four households decide they will decide it against the thing he brought.** The fen did not decide, in Ch 115 or since | Ch 115, 118 |
| **The fieldbook is refused** | on the sixth of next month, in the seed house, in front of eight people, the man of about fifty asked for the book with his reason stated first and the reason was bad and he said so; **Lissa Vale said no**, in one word, and there were four seconds of silence and the silence is in the book and **a silence is not a reason**; the man of about fifty did not ask again, at the door, or that night, and the fen's reason for that is entered and is **not a virtue and is not a decision**; the two standing reasons are given unchanged and a third is added — **an offer and not an invitation: any person who wants to know what her husband wrote may come up the road on their own two feet and ask her in a barn with nobody in it** | Ch 116 |
| **The copy in the coat** | the sub-office copy of the eighteenth of last month was **read twice at the Fennmere counter on the second of next month by a clerk of that office**, with no reason to know it was not his to read and no rule there that told him; Perrin Osse entered the fact with the hour, had the envelope returned on the ordinary post with a line saying it was not addressed to that office, and said the two things she has said twice in four years: **the record of an instrument is not the instrument**, and **nothing in this building will stop me except me** — the refusal clause does not stop her and never was going to, and it is a refusal somebody has to keep by hand every day. **Renner's copy is the safe one because it is his, and that is exactly why it is the one nobody can rely on: a licensed man's copy is a licensed man's, and he can lose the coat and the book, and he can be run over on the fen road in a month, and the fen would then hold three copies and two of them are in its own name and can be declared part three** | Ch 114 |
| **The buyer** | on the seventh of next month at the seventh hour at Cauldron Reach, about fifty, a coat for the wind; **nine shillings the hundredweight, the same four seasons**, at the barn door, weighed in the yard, on the day and not on a day after it; taken as it stands at picking and **not warranted against frost, hail, scab, wind-burn, or any fault of the ground**; for the season and does not return; the buyer is not asking what is in the ground and has not asked in four seasons; **he gives the figure before he is asked for it**; **in four seasons nobody has ever given me a reason, and I have learned not to ask for one**; and **I will not come back after this season** — not a threat and not an opening, because he has four hundred acres of his own on flat watered ground and a shelf nine days from a road is not a place a man builds a trade on | Ch 117 |
| **Merrid Ollvar tells him, unasked, before any figure is agreed** | in about nine sentences, in a yard, with fourteen people in it, at about the eighth hour: the fruit is not ordinary, a row fruited eleven weeks out of season, four men can say what it is and three are in the country and the fourth is four hundred miles off and has not been able to tell her, **and she is not asking him to change his price and she is not asking him to promise to come back** | Ch 117 |
| **The sum, and the batch's largest figure** | **one hundred and nine hundredweight at nine shillings the hundredweight is forty-nine pounds and one shilling**, weighed in the yard with the count said out loud; **more than this holding has taken in since the charter was made**; not more than the repair cost; sixty pounds ten and the four pounds five expressly not in the sum. Done on a bench at Lantern Reach four hundred miles off on the eighth of next month, in Anneke Roos's hand in the ledger, **and the price is not in the fen's book** — the fen has that a figure was read aloud in a yard on a shelf on the seventh, and it has asked for the price to be sent on paper and the clerk has entered that she asked. **The largest number said aloud in this fen for three years is a number the fen cannot check** | Ch 117 |
