| **The household's decision** | **they sold all of it**, at the buyer's price, at the barn door, weighed in the yard, and set aside a fifth for the four houses before anything came out of the stone room, **because a household of four that sells everything has nothing in the stone room on the eleventh of this month, and because she is fifty-four and had the money in her head since the eighth hour and wanted it in a drawer with somebody else's name on the account**; the reason was written on the back of the reading before the money was counted. **No noble refusal, no lesson, nobody treated it as either, and the fen was four hundred miles off and was not asked and was not consulted**; nobody on the shelf told the buyer about the eleven cuts, and that silence is entered, and so is Tova Reed's fee in the same yard, and **the fen has entered that it is going to have to learn to count its silences, because a body that enters some silences and not others has a habit and not a rule** | Ch 117 |
| **Marek Vale's unasked answer** | written on the seventh of next month at about the fifth hour, nine lines, in his own hand in the seed-route ledger, given to a hired man going up the road, and **it is a fact about a row and a date and it is not advice and the reader read it back twice and said so out loud**; the hire went into the compost line and the fen entered that the first of two such hires was asked for and the second was asked for by nobody; **the sheet will arrive about nine days behind a decision already taken and nobody on the shelf is going to be asked to read it** | Ch 117 |
| **The fourth copy** | **not a better copy — a copy in a shape the schedule has no part for, and the shape is a sequence.** Two pages, eight numbered points, the eleven dated faces, the bevel, the healing, the rootwood in a maintenance order over twenty-eight years, the same bevel on the ends, pressed at a mill that is not a family press, three grafts at the core, and the sheet of district paper with an office at the head, a signature at the foot, four lines of which one word is heavier, and the two years. **It names nothing and no one, it is addressed to nobody, there is no *you* in it, nobody is told anything by it, and the four lines that tell a clerk what to enter are not in it and are not going to be in it**; the word *necessary* is a reason a man writes for himself at four in the morning and does not send to anybody | Ch 118 |
| **Why the sequence cannot be sent** | part one is a work and a result, part two a work and a reading, part three a work, its reading, and a direction given to another person; a sequence is **not a result** (it is what was done and in what order), **not a reading** (nobody read a tree in it; a date is not a reading), and **not part three** (no direction); and the form's way out — a holder who cannot classify states so, and the statement is itself a classification — is the trap, because a classification is part three. **So it cannot be sent, cannot be listed, and cannot be mentioned, and the return of six that went on the twenty-eighth is complete and correct** | Ch 118 |
| **The win, and its cost** | four copies into four hands on the tenth, none headed by a holder, none carrying a part, none carrying a name, and **the seventh column not in them, entered as a loss.** The man of about fifty: **we have left a record off a return, and a return of inventories is a document and a document is a statement**; this is the second time the fen has got a good answer out of a form by finding the form cannot hold it, and the fourth time in three years it has kept a thing on a shape, and **a person in four years is going to work out how to ask for a shape.** Tova Reed: **a shape an instrument cannot reach is a good way to live and it is not a way to win**, said four times in three years, and the day comes when four places are not enough | Ch 118 |
| **The question, asked once** | in a barn at about the sixth hour of the tenth of next month with the lamp lit and about nine people in it, by Tova Reed, for herself and not for the body: a man cut eleven branches off one row in one week and dressed every one; a man cut rootwood out of a seam in a particular order for four times in twenty-eight years and pressed it and put a graft in with the waste; a man wrote one word on a sheet and hid the sheet from the node. **He made a list before he started, and nobody knows whether it is still going on, or it is not. Which is it.** **Nobody answered, nobody knew, and the question was not asked of the one man in the room who is dead** | Ch 118 |
| **The March's tin** | **day minus nineteen**: **one hundred and seventy-one days on the tenth of next month**, unmoved, bed at three and a half degrees, **both undertakings standing** — the seed house's that it will not be moved, the trust's that it will not be asked | Ch 118 |
| **The fieldbook's standing, unchanged** | closed, boxed, in the room with a lock at the first house on the shelf, key on Merrid Ollvar's ring, **not coming down that road**, and **may not be opened again in this season**; **nobody asked for it twice in this batch and the only person who asked was refused in one word in front of eight people** | Ch 116, 118 |

## Section-nine notes: twenty-seventh to thirty-first

Twenty-six at the end of Batch 0001 → **twenty-seventh** Ch 109 (the twenty-second), **twenty-eighth** Ch 111 (the twenty-sixth), **twenty-ninth** Ch 113 (the thirtieth), **thirtieth** Ch 116 (the sixth of next month), **thirty-first** Ch 118 (the tenth of next month). Consecutive, with none in Chs 110, 112, 114, 115 or 117. 27 **argument**, sentence carried; 28 **record and the sentence is off** with the reason written underneath; 29 **argument**, sentence carried; 30 **argument**, sentence carried; 31 **argument**, sentence carried. **Stands on twenty-nine of thirty-one since Volume 01, and on four of five in this batch; the one off is off with a reason and the reason is on the page.** The two standing italic lines at the foot of the notes are the manuscript's formula and are kept verbatim — the findings file for Batch 0001 records this and a later close must not spend a finding on it or vary it.

## Fieldbook panel: none spent in this batch

**Batch 0002's one available panel was not spent, and the reason is in the findings file: it must not be spent on a sequence of dates.** The batch's argument is about a record's *shape* and about who has had a record in their hands, and the instrument does not know what either of those is. The panel spent in Ch 101 remains spent and is quoted in no document anywhere in the book. Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 have one each available.

## Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0002

**Marek Vale is Stage 3, Drawwright, unchanged in this batch, and nothing was attempted.**

- **Stage 3 was spent and paid in Ch 106 and no second use is attempted anywhere in Chapters 109–118.** A second draw was put on the table on the fourth of next month, with the standing rule satisfied and nothing forbidding it, and the fen has not decided it and has entered that it has not decided it. The batch's card and lock 4 both forbid the attempt and the attempt does not occur.
- **The rootmark is unchanged: four inches along the inside of the left forearm, branching twice, the color of a healed burn and not a bruise.** The heat in Ch 115's second entry runs past it to the top of the shoulder and **is not a mark.** **No second rootmark appears and the mark is not lengthened.**
- **Two links, three at the outside, a fourth fails and is not attempted.** Unchanged, and the fen is two links down after Ch 105. **No graft, no scion, no union and no new link appears in this batch.** The three grafts in the stack are a dead man's work of an unknown date, are in rootwood, and are not a link and cannot be.
- **A draw is water, heat and rootlight together, through a living path, over about four feet, for about four hours.** No draw at range, no draw without a living path, no draw twice in a week. No return by rootlight at range, no bead, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive, no memory read. **Nobody in this batch sees a vision, hears a dead man, or reads anything out of living wood; the sequence in the bales is read by counting layers with a rule and a lens, which is engineering.**
- **The standing rule of Ch 106 binds him and is now satisfied, and satisfaction is not permission.** It is satisfied in a room, on a day, with a licensed man writing it down, and it says nothing about what comes after the stop, and that hole is now on the page.
- **The Continuity Office has not been named on the page.** An office is named at the head of a sheet that is described in Ch 118's sequence and is not reproduced and is not explained.

## New locks from Volume 03 Batch 0002

- **The leak is five-eighths, unmended, undiagnosed, and is not a mystery.** Measured in a yard on day 171 and in a full yard on day 181 with a reader appointed and the count read out, in the forty-third day since the last entry in the book, and **it does not move and the not-moving is not an answer and not a resolution.** It has no due date and none is to be invented. **A batch may not restate the gap as fourteen days, may not give the road as the cause, may not say the yard was empty, and may not merge the thirty-three days since the last entry in the book with the fourteen days since the party went up on the seventh.**
- **The stack is open and it is dated.** The bevel is in a second place. The sound wood is **twenty-eight years old and inside Merrid Ollvar's own time, in four bands seven years, seven years and six years apart, the last about six years back, with bare years between.** The three grafts are out of the fourth band. **None of this is resolved, and a batch may not say whether the telling is still going on.**
- **Lissa Vale's refusal to open the stack was right and is paid off in public, and her second refusal in a season is the fieldbook and it is entered in one word with four seconds of silence.** She is not forgiven and is not thanked. **Nobody asked her to open the fieldbook in Ch 108 and nobody has asked since, and the one person who asked to see what is in it was refused.**
- **The second draw is on the table and is not attempted and is not decided.** It is not forbidden. **A later batch may attempt it and must then pay for it, and must honour the man at the second house's ninth day, which is the fifteenth of next month, day 195.**
- **The office is not a villain and has never been one.** A clerk read a copy he was not entitled to refuse; the clerk of the counter entered it with the hour and returned the envelope; a records officer said three things in the order she said them. **A correct answer to a correct form is a wall, and the fen's answer to it is a shape the form cannot hold, and the fen has entered that this is the second time and that it is not a victory.**
- **The commercial harvest is decided by the people it belongs to.** The fen was not asked, was not consulted, and the row of eleven cuts and the crate and the money all stay on the record. **A later batch may not have the fen buy a virtue at somebody else's price and may not treat the household's sale as a lesson.**
- **Anneke Roos's sum is forty-nine pounds and one shilling and the price is not in the fen's book, and the largest number this body has said aloud in three years is a number it cannot check.** She has asked for the price on paper.
- **Fourteen and nine days of road are the batch's real distances** and nothing may be quietly compressed: nine up, four down, and the last two days of a descent are the first two days of somebody else's ascent.
- **Merrid Ollvar, Sabin Roell and the man at the second house are the named people of the shelf and the counter and none of them is a member of this body.** The man at the second house is unnamed and is held by function. **No new named member of the cooperative was created.** The crew is unchanged: Ilsa Kern, Jonas Byre, Anneke Roos, Gael Senn, Lissa Vale, Dunmar Fitch, Marek Vale, plus Tova Reed and Coll Renner and the man of about fifty and the man of about seventy.
- **The beat is at the fifteenth, logged the twenty-first of last month, cause not established, nothing written underneath it, not logged since, column open and the fen has not opened it.** The pressure pulse stays undiagnosed and nobody has named it.
- **The well at Cauldron Reach is five feet and two inches and is not coming back on the page.** Four houses are on a day letter. **The ecological debt of Stage 3 is now a relationship on a plateau with dates on it, and it is owed to people, not to a figure.**
- **The Thornwild settlement is not named, the long line is untouched, and the word *seat* does not appear in Chapters 109–118.** The bypass is unbuilt and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. **Nia's cart has not been mentioned in this batch and her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across seven batches.**
- **Sera Quill is not in this batch, is not asked about Brinewake, and her fourth refusal is unnumbered, unasked and may not be numbered.** The confession is Movement 3, Chapters 119–126, and no word of it is spent here.
- **No romance. Nothing tender on the page.** Nothing in this batch is a scene between Marek and Tova; the asking in Ch 115 is asked on behalf of the body in front of fourteen people and neither of them touches the other.
- **The eleven cuts are not resolved.** The two readings stand and neither wins. **A person may say what evidence there is and what there is not; nobody may say the answer.**
- **Ending untouched. No new final enemy.** Rung 4 (the Pale Orchard) has not entered and may not enter before Volume 04. Rung 6 (Iona Vey) is not named.

## Two recorded deviations from the batch's own card, both deliberate

1. **Perrin Osse is *she* in Chapter 114 and not *he*.** The card uses *he*; the character bible and every prior mention in the manuscript use *she* and *her* (Volume 02 Chapters 28, 37, 40 and `state/continuity.md`). **The bible and the prose are the authority on a character's sex and the card's usage is a slip in the card, not a change of canon. A later writer may not move this.**
2. **The part three return was filled in at Lantern Reach and answered there, and the shelf's business is carried by document and by paid messenger.** The card requires the return to be filled in by Gael Senn at the gate, and requires the answer on day 185 in a full yard at Lantern Reach, and requires the household and the man at the second house to be on the shelf; **nine days of road will not put four people on a shelf four days after they are in the fen.** The chapters keep every one of those requirements and reconcile them the only way the geography allows, and **the reconciliation is entered on the page twice — the hire of the messenger in the compost line, and the fen's own statement that a sheet carried nine days behind a decision is a record of what a man said on the seventh and not of anything that happened on it.** A later writer may not quietly undo the reconciliation by putting somebody on that shelf who could not have got there.

## Proposed canon changes — Volume 03 Batch 0002

Three, none of which changes a world rule, a power rule, a relationship lock, or the ending; all three are local instruments and are recorded rather than silently applied.

1. **The leak's practice is now an entry in the fen's book with a person's name against it and no due date** — the first time in this manuscript that a habit has been given an owner after the fact, and the first time a body has entered a practice with the declared reason that a due date on a thing done on most mornings would be a way of saying it was not being done.
2. **The fourth copy in the shape of a sequence** — a class of record with no part in the fourth part of a schedule of records held, held by nobody and classified by nobody, distributed in four hands. The fourth time in three years the fen has kept a thing on a shape, and the fen has entered the count.
3. **The day letter for four houses at Cauldron Reach** — a domestic water-sharing arrangement entered in a household's own bad ledger with the days on it, and a debt entered as a debt and not a favour. It is the first instrument in this manuscript that is not a fen document and not a district document, and it binds nobody, which is the manuscript's standing condition on all of its instruments.

---

# VOLUME 03 — BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 119–128, MOVEMENT 3

**Volume 03, *The Memory Orchard*, Movement 3, *What Sera Took Out*. Ten chapters, days 191–210. The volume's outline is `outline/volume-03.md`. Its governing prompt is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. Volume 01 and Volume 02 are closed. Nothing below reopens either volume and nothing below moves a date the Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 pass settled.**

## The dating rule inside this batch, which is the whole of its arithmetic

Days 1–30 the first month, 31–60 the second, 61–90 the third, 91–120 the fourth, 121–150 the fifth, **151–180 the sixth, 181–210 the seventh.** Day 191 is the eleventh of the seventh and day 210 is the thirtieth, the last reading day of that month. **There is no month turn inside this batch, so there is one naming table and not two**, which is the first time in two batches that the manuscript has run a full ten chapters without a turn in it. From a scene inside days 191–210:

| Day | Named, in Chapters 119–128 |
|---|---|
| 1–30 | *a month seven months back* |
| 31–60 | *a month six months back* |
| 61–90 | *a month five months back* |
| 91–120 | *a month four months back* |
| 121–150 | *a month three months back* |
| 151–180 | *last month* |
| 181–210 | *of this month*, *the first*, *the second*, or the bare ordinal |

**October is the first month, is seven months back by day 191, and is named nowhere in the batch.** *March* is not a month; the only capitalized *March* in the ten chapters is *the March's tin*, which is the Salt March trust's seed.

## Volume 03 Batch 0003 day clock, confirmed against the drafted chapters

Ch 119 days 191–192 (**Mon the eleventh, Tue the twelfth**) · Ch 120 days 193–194 (Wed the thirteenth, Thu the fourteenth) · Ch 121 days 195–196 (**Fri the fifteenth, Sat the sixteenth**) · Ch 122 days 197–198 (**Sun the seventeenth, Mon the eighteenth**) · Ch 123 days 199–200 (Tue the nineteenth, Wed the twentieth) · Ch 124 days 201–202 (Thu the twenty-first, Fri the twenty-second) · Ch 125 days 203–204 (**Sat the twenty-third, Sun the twenty-fourth**) · Ch 126 days 205–206 (Mon the twenty-fifth, Tue the twenty-sixth) · Ch 127 days 207–208 (Wed the twenty-seventh, Thu the twenty-eighth) · Ch 128 days 209–210 (**Fri the twenty-ninth, Sat the thirtieth**).

**The batch runs days 191–210 with no gap and no chapter reaches past its own range.** Day 190 was the tenth of the seventh month, a Sunday, and is Batch 0002's last day. Day 191 is a Monday, which follows from day 181 being a Friday. **Day 211 is the first of the eighth month and is outside this batch; Movement 4 opens there.**

**The three checkable weekday statements on the page are:** the man at the second house's arrival at the tenth hour of the fifteenth, a **Friday**; the middle reading of the well house door and the occupier's own well reading on the sixteenth, a **Saturday**; and the last reading of the door of the seventh month on the thirtieth, a **Saturday**. All three agree with the table. **A fourth statement is also on the page and also agrees: the statement is read in a room on the seventeenth, which is a Sunday, and she named the day for that reason and said so.**

**The inherited readings all still travel as day numbers, and every one of them is now on the page under its correct name for this batch:** the well is nine feet on day 136 (*a month three months back*), five feet and a half on 164 (*the fourteenth of last month*, the extra reading asked for in front of witnesses), five feet five inches on 166, five feet and four inches on 170, five feet and two inches on 180, **and five feet and three inches on 196, which is new and is the sixth reading and the first ordinary one on this batch's side of the table.** The leak's book entries are days 121, 135 and 138 and it was measured in a yard on days 171 and 181; **the figure quoted in Ch 128 resolves to day 181 and to nothing else, and the forty-third day and forty-two mornings in between are Ch 114's own figures quoted forward, not a new interval.** The beat is logged on day 171, *the twenty-first of last month*.

**One geographic decision this batch had to make and a later writer must honour.** **The man at the second house came off the plateau road on the sixth of this month, day 186, and was in the Lantern Reach yard on the fifteenth, day 195, which is the ninth day he named in Ch 116, and the road is four days.** The fen has entered that it does not know who paid the four hundred miles and has decided not to ask, and has entered that it does not know what a man did with five of the nine days and is not going to ask that either. **He is in the fen from day 195 to day 210 and nowhere else, and he goes on the afternoon cart on the thirtieth, and on the thirtieth he has been in the yard fifteen days.** **Nothing on the shelf after the thirtieth of last month is narrated from inside a person standing in that yard except the fourth-hour reading of the well on the sixteenth, which is narrated the way Ch 117 narrated the buyer's day: from outside, with the figure and the hands and the hour on it, and with the four-day sheet hired for eight shillings and entered in the compost line the day before the man was hired.** **The road is nine days up and four down and no batch may make it shorter.**

## Numbers and records established in Chapters 119–128

| Fact | Value | Established |
|---|---|---|
| **Sera Quill's arrival** | on foot on the eleventh of this month at about the ninth hour, off the Glassward road and down the fen road, the last of it alone; gave her name once; **fifty-six, retired root engineer**; not a member of anything, not asked, not made one; **the name is in the fen's own book in two places, one of them at the head of a rider's letter a man in this yard has seen and did not say aloud, and the other a line in a ledger in a stone house four hundred miles off** | Ch 119 |
| **The three things, in her order** | (1) no fee, and the reason: **a paid witness is a witness somebody bought**, and she would rather be argued with in public and by name; (2) not a member and not asking; (3) **a room and not a hearing**, and the clerk's entry: *a room is not a hearing, a hearing is not a room, a room is not a record* | Ch 119 |
| **The fee, refused and paid** | not offered, **entered**, paid inside four minutes, read back with the hour; **three shillings and eightpence for a fortnight's keep** at Anneke Roos's two-summers-old figure for one woman on her own feet, expressly not a wage, plus **eleven shillings for the licensed man** for three days at his own figure; **fifteen shillings and eightpence**, about a tenth of the four pounds five in the tin; **a second blanket at twopence entered immediately and the smallest line in the compost line for four years**; the second half of the fortnight entered on the twenty-fourth and a second week at the same figure entered on the twenty-fifth, **not voted on** | Ch 119, 122, 126 |
| **Anneke Roos's sentence on the fee** | **this holding refused a free offer in writing in one line on the fifteenth of last month and is now paying a stranger to tell it something, and both halves of that are in the same book and the fen does not get to be only one of them.** Entered on the eleventh, repeated on the seventeenth and on the twenty-fifth, and the reader is to enter any thanks said to that woman and who said it | Ch 119, 122, 126 |
| **What she kept** | **nothing.** No copy, no extract, no log, no note, no private ledger, no letter to anybody about it. The reason given: a woman who keeps a copy keeps a reason, and the reason is never the reason she gives; and in nineteen years there has never been a moment where the having of it would have helped anybody. **She told the clerk she has it — a piece of a machine that is not a document and is not paper — which is more than she has told anybody in nineteen years** | Ch 120, 122 |
| **The refusal of a document** | asked for by the clerk with the reason stated first; refused **in eleven words** — *No, because a woman who keeps a copy keeps a reason* — with the reason inside the eleven and nothing after them, the clerk waited four seconds for an addition and there was none | Ch 120 |
| **The terms, seven lines** | what she did, in order, in a room, on the seventeenth, in her own hand, a page held by a steward and a licensed man present; read out whole and read back, the figures as figures, a stumble means she says it again; **she will not be asked what she felt and will not be asked whether it was right**; **she will say an office's name only if the name is on a paper in front of her, and will not explain it**; not a member; a room and not a hearing. **Read back once in a room with nobody in it and once in a room with about nine people in it, and the two agreed word for word and the reader compared them** | Ch 120 |
| **The fourth refusal, unnumbered** | *who told you to do it*, asked in a yard on the fourteenth by the reader of this body, who stated his reason first: **if there was somebody else then the largest thing this fen is holding is not a woman and a piece of a machine, it is an office, and an office can be answered and answers correctly in seven days.** Not answered. Entered as asked and not answered. **Not numbered, has not been numbered in three years and is not going to be; a refusal is not a currency, it has not been paid for and it buys nothing** | Ch 120 |
| **The man at the second house's day** | in the yard at the tenth hour of the fifteenth, **nine days off a four-day road**; **the fare was not eleven shillings a day and was not paid out of the compost line and the fen does not know who paid it; not asked, and the decision entered**; anger entered as a fact, *you spent a bucket*, said once and not again that day, no reason asked for, nobody asked him to leave the yard and nobody asked him to explain himself twice | Ch 121 |
| **The answer, four entries** | (1) **the draw of the thirteenth of last month** in his own hand, four feet and four hours, with its cost: fever inside two hours, three fingers gone from the second joint until the second morning, taste gone entire and sour not back on the twentieth; (2) **Coll Renner's, in his own book, *not answering for a draw***; (3) **the undertaking made standing in a barn at about the sixth hour of the twelfth of last month, before the thing was done, in those words, read back on the fourteenth in a yard with nine people in it**; (4) **the thirtieth of last month: three and a half feet is mine and the other half inch is not, and it stays the ground's in every book.** Not an apology and no word of one; no defense offered | Ch 121 |
| **Did not accept. Did not refuse.** | said by the man at the second house, and entered **in the order they were said in and not in the order a person would prefer them in**; he took the second hour of that morning and said so before he sat down | Ch 121 |
| **The well on the shelf, sixth reading** | **five feet and three inches** at the fourth hour of the sixteenth of this month, by the occupier, in her own yard, in her own hand and by her own nail, said out loud before it was written down, which is not her rule and is the fen's and she took it off the fen without being asked. **Up an inch in sixteen days and the inch is a household's decision and not a figure.** Shortfall **three feet and nine inches**: **three and a half feet the draw's, entered against a man; half an inch the ground's, entered against nobody; two hands, one ledger, one day. No entry anywhere for that well coming back** | Ch 121, 123, 128 |
| **The count of the seventh column** | **twenty-five on the sixteenth** in a full yard with a stranger in it, **the first middle reading of the season made with the body in its own yard and with somebody in it who is not of the fen**; **twenty-six on the thirtieth** in a yard of about twenty-five. Twenty-four was on the first of this month, so the count has gone up by two in twenty-nine days and neither was caused by anything said in a room | Ch 121, 128 |
| **The count checked by a stranger** | she counted the column off the board from the back of the yard and said **twenty-five**, and noted that the head of the column still said twenty-four and called it a practice and not a fault; the man at the second house read the whole column aloud with his finger, **neither of them a clerk, neither knowing the other's name, and the two counts agreeing** | Ch 121 |
| **The statement** | nine lines, her own hand, read out whole on the seventeenth in a room with the bay doors shut, a page held by the steward, a licensed man present with **nothing on his page but a date**, two witnesses appointed standing up before a word was read (Jonas Byre, for a thing said once in a room is a practice; Gael Senn, for the standing that the person who keeps the books hears a document before it goes in, never once exercised, and this is not the place to start it). **It is a statement of records and not of events and she says so on her own first line: she was not present for any of it and saw no collapse and no body** | Ch 122 |
| **What she admits** | **the Continuity Office, named once and only on paper, as the office at the head of the district's index of relay designs, and she will not say it again and will not say what it means and was asked nothing about it.** A Crown safety relay of her own design to the western salt corridor, a relay that sits at a collar, watches the flow and says out loud that the flow has gone above a line it is not to go above, **which could have isolated a pruning pulse before it reached the three heartroots**; designed, built, tested and signed by her and it worked. **The red-line warning removed by her hand, on the fourteenth of that month, at about the second hour, alone, in a room with a hand lamp, bridge out and fuse out and cover back on, eleven minutes, and nobody in the world saw her do it.** The maintenance log for that season and the one before altered twice in the same hand she has always used, on the sixteenth and the twenty-ninth, and the words put in were **routine recalibration.** A report signed against salt creep and weather **in which every figure is true and the corridor is not mentioned and the reason it is not mentioned is that she wrote it that way.** **Four thousand two hundred died in the first winter; nine thousand displaced; and she will not have the two added together because they are not the same kind of thing** | Ch 122 |
| **Nine** | **she was not at the Quiet Break; it is a hundred and twenty-one years old; she was not born; she has never claimed it and is not claiming it, and any person in any room who sets it beside the four above should take it out again** | Ch 122 |
| **The question asked and not answered** | **the engineer of record, in a room, on the day: *how long did it take you to decide*.** Not answered. The answer is not going to be asked for, and the reason she would not give it is not entered and is not going to be asked for either. **The reader's finding: it is the first figure in two volumes this body has wanted more than anything and cannot have, and a thing a woman says in a room that a man cannot check is not safer for being unread, it is only quieter, and quiet is not the same as sound** | Ch 122 |
| **The cost, entered as a cost** | **a woman of fifty-six came into this yard on the eleventh at the ninth hour and the seventeenth is the seventh day, and she has given six days of a fortnight's living to a room in a fen and has asked for nothing and is not to be thanked and cannot be** | Ch 122 |
| **The statement changed nothing** | on the eighteenth the man of about fifty asked what in this holding the statement changed; Lissa Vale read the list out of the ledger in the open and counted twice: route, door, books, shut drawer, fieldbook, eleven cuts, stack and bevel, compost line, well, beat, leak, second draw. **Not one of them moved, and the reason is that the list was the work and the statement was never going to be the work** | Ch 122 |
| **The relay fragment** | given into Marek Vale's right hand on the nineteenth at about the ninth hour, in front of about eleven people, **and nothing else**; a collar section about the length of a hand, steel and brass, an empty socket with the terminals standing proud, a cut on one side with the metal displaced from the cut and not toward it, and a bead set in it. **Coll Renner says out loud, before touching anything, that he is not going to touch the bead, and the clerk enters that he said it.** Four facts and no finding, and his standing line: **a reading of a piece of metal is not a witness, and I cannot tell you who held the blade.** The rest of that relay is **four hundred miles away in a district's storage under a schedule of stored plant she has never seen and will not describe and will not number** | Ch 123 |
| **The naming standing** | **her name on the page first and the engineer's under it, and the reason entered: a licensed engineer's name on a thing is a promise that the thing is somebody's to answer for, and the somebody is her, and she is a woman of fifty-six with no employer, no district behind her and no office that would stand in front of her at a hearing, and if this piece of metal is ever a matter for anybody there has to be a person on it who cannot be reached by a letter. There is no certificate for a thing like this in any district in the world** | Ch 123 |
| **A fragment cannot be copied** | four attempts in six hours, all in a yard, all entered: a scribe's description, **refused by the steward standing up**; a tracing, **refused by the licensed man, *the shape of it is the least of it***; taking it apart in three pieces, **refused by the engineer of record, who would rather be asked that than be asked what he did with the nineteen years it was in a drawer**; a letter to a licensed man two days off, written and not sent. **It is not copied, not traced, not taken apart and not sent, and it is in this building** | Ch 123 |
| **Anneke Roos's arithmetic, four sums** | a red line is **the announcement of a stop**, not a stop; the western salt corridor **a hundred and forty miles**; a full-rate pulse runs it in **about seventy minutes**; **an isolation takes about twenty minutes to close by hand with a man standing at it and holds about four hours**; **four thousand two hundred died in the first winter, in eleven weeks**; **nine thousand is a count of registrations and is a floor and not a total, and she will not do a sum with a floor in it**; the nearest high ground **thirty-one miles** off and **about eleven hours** on salt flat in the dark at three miles an hour. **The sum: four hours against eleven, the difference seven, and she cannot finish it, because no version of the end of that sum reduces four thousand two hundred by a known amount. She asks for the figures to be sent, the clerk enters that she asked, and they have not come. Her sentence: a figure in a yard that four people out of five were not in the room for, and the fifth is a licensed man with a clip** | Ch 123 |
| **The sealed compensation record** | **named as sealed and not opened, held by the office at the head of the district's index of relay designs and not said out loud in a yard since the eighteenth**; **people in it by name whose names are not on the page and are not going to be, and the reason is standing and not privacy: this fen has no standing over any of them and neither has she**; one of the names is a name she put in a bed nine days after the pulse. The entry is **the fact of a refusal and not its terms**, and the terms are written down by nobody, held by nobody, and are not going to be asked for | Ch 124 |
| **The new thing** | **she is the only living person who knows what is in it.** Said out loud in a yard by the man of about fifty, on his own word and not on anybody's, without asking her; **she neither confirms nor denies, because a confirmation and a denial would both be documents and she has made none in nineteen years.** **A body cannot check this and cannot ask for it and cannot cite the fact that it exists without making a claim it cannot support; the word *only* is the word doing the damage. The line stands as a fact about a day and not as a finding, and the cost of it is that it grows** | Ch 124 |
| **The three walls** | **a record nobody can produce cannot corroborate a statement and cannot be used to attack one — a wall on both sides, and it is the same wall as the part three condition and the same wall as the shape the schedule has no part for, and this body has now built three of them in three weeks and every one of them is correct, and the count is entered.** The man of about seventy: **a wall that is put up and held is a wall, and a wall that grows is a debt with no date on it**, and there is a fourth one coming with no name and no number and no day, and he entered that he noticed it and the fen did not enter what it was | Ch 124 |
| **The silences, counted** | **the question of what her life was like afterwards was available to anybody in that yard and was taken by nobody and is not going to be, and the count of how many times this fen has entered that sentence is entered and is now three: first at a stack against a north wall, second in a yard about a day letter, third here.** The same sentence is entered a second time on the twenty-fifth of this month, about the work order, in the same batch and for the same reason | Ch 124, 126 |
| **The midpoint turn, in her words** | **the pruning on the western salt corridor was not a mistake that went wrong, it was a decision that went right. The ground that was pruned hardest is the ground that came back and the ground that was left alone is the ground that did not come back.** Said in the flat voice she uses for reading a table, not as an argument, and she is not arguing it was right. **The people who did it had a reason and the reason worked and the reason is not on anything that survives, and a man who cut eleven branches off one row in one week this season is not a mystery and was probably not a fool** | Ch 125 |
| **Why it is not a finding** | she kept nothing, the figures were asked for on the twentieth and have not come, and it stands as **a woman's account of a table in a district that does not answer to this one, given in a yard, on a Saturday, entered with the hour** | Ch 125 |
| **Marek's mechanism** | a graft carries the thing put in and loses the thing that was there; a tree does the work it was told to do in the order it was told to do it. **Eleven cuts, nine read and two not read and not guessed at, one row, one week, this season, in an order, dressed eleven times by a man who came back on the sixth day; and the reading of the fifth of last month says the row went on being a tree, so the row is not damaged and is not a scar.** **The question in this body's book changes: it was *is the telling still going on* and he asks for it struck out of his own mouth in front of witnesses, and it is now *whose order is it*, which is worse, and the fen has entered that it is worse.** He will not line the orders up in a yard and imply they are the same | Ch 125 |
| **Tova Reed's third statement of the same argument** | **a mechanism is not a verification**; soundness is not a hand; a mechanism is sound in the same way for every order in the book, sound or not, and the distance between a mechanism and a hand is the whole of this volume and nobody in that yard can close it. **Eleven cuts has stopped being a mystery in this holding's language and started being an example, and an example is the cheapest thing in a book** | Ch 125 |
| **The work order put in front of her** | **as a fact and not as a question**, and the putting of it was Lissa Vale's and took six minutes and she told nobody. **Four lines in the steward's hand, copied on the sixteenth of last month at about the fourth hour from a sheet of three in a stone barn, and the page itself is in a locked room four hundred miles off and is not coming down that road this season.** Beside them, not read out: district paper of a shape not used here for a long time, an office's name at the head, **the year of the shaft**, four lines in one hand with one word heavier than the others, and the hand making the letter *W* a way a licensed man has seen once. **She reads it twice, goes back to the first line on the second reading, chooses the four lines and not the copy of the handwriting, and the fen enters that she chose them and does not enter why** | Ch 126 |
| **Three refusals, not one** | *yes, I have seen paper like that*, and then **refused where, refused when, and refused the name of the office at the head of the sheet**, given in that order, not asked for, entered as three and not as one, **and nobody asked her what it is or whose it is or when it was written or who wrote it or what the word means or whether the word was right** | Ch 126 |
| **Coll Renner on a hand** | **a hand can be described and a hand cannot be dated by anybody who has not got the original in front of him, and he has not got the original, he has got four lines in another person's hand.** He will go no further than *trade* — a man who writes in a book does not tell a clerk what to enter about a node — and asks for the word in his own hand and for the page read back by a man who is not a licensed man | Ch 126 |
| **Marek's position, which is the position of the volume** | **on the eighteenth of last month he entered that he is not going to be able to find out whether his father was right, and he is not unsaying it.** The room is shut, the key is on somebody's ring, the book is not coming down that road this season, and he is not going to be the man who makes the steward hear the refusal twice. **What he has instead is a sequence, a word, and a woman who has seen paper like that, entered in one line so that a person in four years does not have to sort them out, and none of the three is a reason.** And: **a body that cannot tell a father from a piece of paper will be told what it wants by the first man who comes up a road with a bag; that man came down that road four days ago with nothing in a bag, and the sentence still stands and stands harder than it did a month ago** | Ch 126 |
| **Where the fragment lives** | the piece itself in the keeping of the engineer of record, whose name stands second on the page on which the first name stands. **A description of it in the fen's own hand, naming nothing and no one, with no part stated and no district holding number: first place a drawer in the well house, which is the one room a stranger may walk into at any hour and which the fen has entered is a hiding place with a lid and was given that because the other thing is worse; second place a person, named once in a room on the twenty-eighth and not entered, who is not of the fen, is not a member of anything, has not been asked, and will not be told anything until somebody asks her.** **A copy held by a person whom nobody can name is not a copy anybody can find, and the fen wanted a thing it could find in nine years' time and has decided it would rather have one it cannot** | Ch 127 |
| **The fifth count** | the fifth time in three years this body has kept a thing on a shape, said out loud and **not called a victory**, and **the day four places are not enough is coming and the day one person is not enough came about two weeks ago.** The fifth clause's one line of refusal is read out in a yard on the twenty-seventh, because a body that forgets why it refused a free offer is a body that is about to accept one | Ch 127 |
| **Tova Reed's decision is the same decision** | she names the person; the fen entered on the fifteenth of last month that the question of where her own thing came from was available to anybody in that yard and was taken by nobody and will not be taken. **The two decisions are the same decision and neither of them is about the other, and she refuses in a room with nine people in it to make anything of that, and if she had been going to she would have done it on a day when nobody was asking her about a collar** | Ch 127 |
| **The road to the shelf** | **four days, one way, and it is the fen's own standing and it is load-bearing for the whole geography.** The man at the second house took **nine** days over it because he walked off the plateau and down with a household, and the fen entered the distinction and did not enter who paid. **A hired man on a change of horses does the four days and a round trip is eight days of it** | Ch 121, and every hired man in this batch and the next |
| **The rate of a hired man** | **four shillings a day, and a whole hire is thirty-two shillings**, entered **four on the day he is hired and the remainder when he is paid off**. Batch 0003's man was hired on the **twelfth of the seventh month**, four days before the figure he was sent for existed, paid off on the **sixteenth**, and the fen entered that **it did not have thirty-two shillings and took them out of the compost line anyway**. **A chapter may not enter a cheaper hire and may not call the four shillings a day a hundredweight price** | Ch 121, 122, 123, 128; the Batch 0004 lock |
| **The keep, closed** | three shillings and eightpence for a fortnight, in two halves, the second week entered on the **twenty-fifth** and not voted on; eleven shillings for the licensed man; twopence for a second blanket. **Entered on the eleventh as fourteen shillings and tenpence, and the whole fortnight as eighteen shillings and sixpence.** **She was here from the eleventh to the thirtieth, which is six days over a fortnight, and those six days were entered on the thirtieth at one shilling and sevenpence, a day being a little over three pence at her figure and the fen refusing a fraction and rounding a person up** — and the reason they were not entered on the twenty-fifth is entered with them | Ch 119, 122, 126, 128 |
| **The standing figures on the thirtieth** | the leak **five-eighths**, two rules, pump on, into the housing and not into the pit, the pit not up, **read at the seventh hour of the first of this month and not measured since**, no due date and none to be invented, **nobody has looked for a cause and none is entered**; the beat **the fifteenth, the twenty-first of last month, nine in the quarter hour with one run of three, at the rail, cause not established, licensed person present none, nothing written underneath it, not logged since, column open, and the fen entered that it has not opened it now with a witness in the yard**; the boards **one hundred and forty-four**, day minus sixty-six; the March's tin **one hundred and ninety-one days**, day minus nineteen, unmoved, three and a half degrees, both undertakings standing, **the drawer not opened this month and not to be**; the standing rule in force, two unreconciled accounts of the same arm, **a second draw on a table undecided for twenty-five days**; the well at five feet and three inches | Ch 128 |
| **The handoff, day 211, outside this batch** | **one, the seventh month's compost line**, which is not in the book and this holding does not know it, on the first of next month; **two, the day letter**, four houses on a shelf, one drawing each in turn, looked at in daylight at the well with whoever is standing there, decided by the households and not by the fen; **three, not a date and a fact: the man at the second house is in this yard since the fifteenth, this fen does not know what he is doing here and has not asked and is not going to, and that sentence was read back twice and then a third time at his own asking** | Ch 128 |
| **The man at the second house's last word in this fen** | **a man can be answered and not accept it, and go on being in a yard, and that this is not a threat and is not an opening. The bucket is a bucket, and a well at the fourth house, and a stone windlass, and a woman of fifty-four who read a nail out loud in front of strangers because a man from nine miles off told her to.** He has not moved the day he put in the fen's book in a barn before he came down here, and knowing more is not a reason to move a day | Ch 128 |

## Section-nine notes: thirty-second to thirty-sixth

Thirty-first at the end of Batch 0002 → **thirty-second** Ch 120 (the fourteenth), **thirty-third** Ch 122 (the eighteenth), **thirty-fourth** Ch 124 (the twenty-second), **thirty-fifth** Ch 127 (the twenty-eighth), **thirty-sixth** Ch 128 (the thirtieth). Consecutive, with none in Chs 119, 121, 123, 125 or 126. 32 **record**, sentence off, with the reason written underneath — *a term agreed between two people is not an argument*; 33 **argument**, sentence carried; 34 **argument**, sentence carried; 35 **argument**, sentence carried; 36 **argument**, sentence carried. **Stands on thirty-five of thirty-six since Volume 01, and on four of five in this batch; the one off is off with a reason and the reason is on the page.** Ch 125 has **no** note and the reason is entered underneath it, that a question asked in public and not answered is not an argument. The two standing italic lines at the foot of the notes are the manuscript's formula and are kept verbatim.

## Fieldbook panel: none spent in this batch

**Batch 0003's one available panel was not spent, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed.** The batch's whole argument is about a shape a record can be kept on, about who is holding what, and about a woman who brought nothing to publish. The instrument does not know what a record's shape is, does not know who is holding it, and **on the only object in the batch that it could plausibly be spent on — a collar section of a relay with a bead in it — Coll Renner says out loud, in a yard, that he is not going to touch the bead and that he cannot read intent in metal, and a panel there would be the book answering a question nobody asked.** The panel spent in Ch 101 remains spent and is quoted in no document anywhere in the book. Batches 0004 and 0005 have one each available.

## Stage and contact accounting after Batch 0003

**Marek Vale is Stage 3, Drawwright, unchanged in this batch, and nothing was attempted.**

- **Stage 3 was spent and paid in Ch 106 and no second use is attempted anywhere in Chapters 119–128.** The second draw is named in Ch 128 as still on a table and undecided for eighteen days. **The man at the second house's day was answered in the first third of this batch, which was the precondition, and the fen has not decided the second draw and has entered that it has not.**
- **The rootmark is unchanged: four inches along the inside of the left forearm, branching twice, the color of a healed burn and not a bruise.** A woman of fifty-six looked at it on the eleventh of this month and said nothing about it, and the fen entered that she said nothing and did not enter why. **The mark is not lengthened and no second mark appears.**
- **Two links, three at the outside, a fourth fails and is not attempted.** Unchanged. **No graft, no scion, no union and no new link appears in this batch.** The relay fragment is nineteen years old, is in another district's storage, and is not a link and cannot be.
- **A draw is water, heat and rootlight together, through a living path, over about four feet, for about four hours.** No draw at range, no draw without a living path, no draw twice in a week. No return by rootlight at range, no bead, no human living anchor, no seedheart, no caretaker link, no deep archive, **no memory read.** **Nobody in this batch sees a vision, hears a dead man, or reads anything out of living wood, and the relay is a piece of metal read with a clip and a lens by a licensed man who says he cannot read intent in it.**
- **The standing rule of Ch 106 binds him and is satisfied, and satisfaction is not permission.** It was read out again in Ch 128 and is in force, and it says nothing whatever about what comes after the stop.
- **The Continuity Office is named once, on paper, in the statement of the seventeenth, as the office at the head of the district's index of relay designs.** It is not explained, it is not in narration, and it is referred to afterwards only as *the office named at the head of the district's index of relay designs*. **A later chapter may not explain it, expand it or put it in a document in any other sense.**

## New locks from Volume 03 Batch 0003

- **Sera Quill is on the page, in person, unpaid, and she is not forgiven and is not thanked and cannot be thanked for being readable. She is not a member of this body, was not asked, and may not be made one.** She has given the statement, the fragment and the arithmetic and nothing else. **Her refusal of a fee is a fact on the page and it does not buy anything.**
- **The statement cannot be corroborated and is entered as uncorroborated, with the reason.** It is the only statement in this manuscript with no document behind any part of it. **A later batch may not find a document, may not produce one, and may not have anybody corroborate it.**
- **The sealed compensation record stays sealed. The entry is the fact of a refusal and not its terms. The names in it are not on the page and may not be put on it.** She is the only living person who knows what is in it, that fact is entered on the reader's word and not on hers, and **the cost of it grows rather than being held.**
- **The fragment cannot be copied, traced, taken apart or sent.** It is in the engineer of record's keeping. **Its description is in a drawer in the well house and with a person who is not named in this body's book and may not be named in it.**
- **The midpoint question has changed and may not be changed back.** *Is the telling still going on* was struck out of Marek Vale's own mouth in front of witnesses on the twenty-third of this month. **The question is now *whose order is it*, and it is worse, and the fen has entered that it is worse. Nobody may say the telling is still going on and nobody may say it has stopped, and a person may say what the evidence is and what it is not.** The eleven cuts are not resolved and have stopped being a mystery and become an instance.
- **The fourth refusal is spent, unnumbered, and it may not be numbered.** It has not been numbered in three years and is not going to be.
- **The man at the second house has been answered and has not accepted the answer and has not refused it, both in the book in the order they were said in. He was angry on the fifteenth and the anger is entered and not a scene of humiliation. He is not a member of this body and has not been asked.**
- **The reader of this body has read one sentence back three times, at the man's own asking, to be certain the page was clean.** That is entered and is a fact about the reader and not a virtue.
- **The work order is in two hands and neither of them can say whether the word was true.** Nobody asked Sera Quill whose hand it is and nobody asked whether the word was right, and the fen has entered that nobody asked. **There is no reconciliation in this batch and there was none available.**
- **The counting of silences is now three and is entered as the count.** A later batch may enter it a fourth time and may not use it as a substitute for asking.
- **No month is named anywhere in Chapters 119–128** and no document in the batch carries one. October is seven months back by day 191 and is named nowhere. *March* is not a month; the only capitalized *March* is *the March's tin*.
- **The word *seat* does not appear.** The Thornwild settlement is not named, the long line is untouched, and the bypass is unbuilt and Ivo Tarrant's four men have not come back. The collie is not interpreted, the weir animal does not return, and Marek has still not walked the low quarter's own map. The envelope is sealed. **Nia's cart has not been mentioned in this batch and her letter is sealed in a shut drawer, unopened and undiscussed, now across eight batches and about a hundred and fifty days of season.**
- **The fieldbook was not opened and nobody asked for it a second time.** The one person who asked in this season was refused in one word in front of eight people on the sixth of this month, and in Ch 126 the work order reaches the table **without the book**, by way of four lines copied out on the sixteenth of last month, and the chapter says in terms that the room is shut and the book is not coming down that road this season. **A second asking is not a beat and may not be written.**
- **The leak is five-eighths, unmended, undiagnosed, and is not a mystery.** Last measured in the book on the first of this month. **A batch may not restate the gap, may not give an interval for it other than the forty-third day and forty-two mornings in between that Ch 114 entered, may not merge it with anything, may not say the yard was empty, may not say the figure has grown and may not say it has not.**
- **The beat is at the fifteenth, logged on the twenty-first of last month, cause not established, nothing written underneath it, not logged since, column open, and the fen has entered that it has not opened it with a witness in the yard.**
- **The pressure pulse stays undiagnosed and nobody has named it. The third exchange requirement is still undescribed and may not be guessed at.**
- **The well at Cauldron Reach is five feet and three inches and is not coming back on the page.** Three and a half feet is entered against a man and half an inch against the ground, in two hands in one ledger. Four houses are on a day letter and it is looked at on the first of next month. **No chapter may predict a return and no chapter may say it will not return.**
- **The commercial harvest stays the household's. Nobody may turn it into a lesson and nobody may have the fen buy a virtue at somebody else's price. The price is not in the fen's book and Anneke Roos asked for it on paper, and it was not sent.**
- **The office is not a villain and has never been one, and no officer of any district lies, obstructs or is corrupt in this batch, because no officer is on the page.**
- **No romance. Nothing tender on the page.** Nothing in this batch is a scene between Marek and Sera Quill, and there is nothing to forgive anybody for.
- **Lissa Vale is not forgiven and is not thanked.** She refused a fee and paid it and said out loud that a body which does a thing twice and says nothing has not changed; she copied four lines out of a dead man's book; she put a page in front of a woman and then stood at a bench for four minutes while nobody looked at her, and entered that herself, and it is the only entry in this body's book a person has written about herself while standing in the room where it happened. **Tova Reed is not thanked and refused in a room with nine people in it to make anything of anything.**
- **Kellan Rusk is not in this batch, and the words *became* and *redeemed* are prohibited for him only.** **The earlier claim in this file that those two words do not appear at all was false and is withdrawn: *became* is in Ch 127, in a sentence about a compliment turning into a standing, and *redeemed* does not appear in the batch.** The lock is Rusk's and has always been Rusk's; it is not a ban on the words in the mouth of anybody else.
- **Brinewake is reserved, not prohibited.** The name is not on the page anywhere in Chapters 99–128 and **may not be put in anybody's mouth in any chapter of Volume 03.** It is canon in the bible and in the series, ending and volume outlines, and **Volume 04's restitution beat has to have the name, and the only two things that can carry it are her and the sealed compensation record** — one mouth that cannot be checked and one record that does not open in this volume. **Do not let a later batch resolve this by writing the name into a document. See the review-pass section below for the full decision.**
- **Ending untouched. No new final enemy.** Rung 4 (the Pale Orchard) has not entered and may not enter before Volume 04. Rung 6 (Iona Vey) is not named.

## Three recorded deviations from the batch's own card, all deliberate and all checkable

1. **The cost of the statement is entered as six days, not four.** The card says *a woman of fifty-six has given four days of a fortnight's living to a room in a fen*. **She came into the yard on the eleventh and the statement was read on the seventeenth, and the seventeenth is the seventh day, so six days of the fortnight are gone and four would be wrong on the page.** The card's sentence is otherwise kept word for word, and **the number is now six and no chapter may re-date it to four.**
2. **The man at the second house's ninth day is Ch 128's *fifteenth of this month*, not the *fifteenth of last month*.** The card for Ch 128 says the fen entered the fact *in the fifteenth of last month*. **The entry was made in Ch 121 on day 195, which is the fifteenth of the seventh month, and the fifteenth of last month is day 165, which is a Saturday inside Batch 0001 and carries the district records officer's arrival, and it has nothing to do with him.** The manuscript reads the fifteenth of this month in both places and **a later chapter may not move it.**
3. **The sentence the man of about seventy repeats in Ch 127 is given with the twenty-second of last month, not the fifth.** The card says *the sentence he said on the fifth of last month about a leak at a hard standing*. **The fifth of last month is Ch 101 and his sentence there is four words long — *write down what you did not find* — and there is no leak in Ch 101. The leak at a hard standing and the sentence about a rule a person has to remember are Ch 109, the twenty-second of last month.** Ch 127 gives **both**, in order and correctly dated, and then gives the new one: *the second copy of anything has to be a person, a person is not a place and a person cannot be put on a return.* **The new sentence is the card's and it is on the page word for word.**

## Proposed canon changes — Volume 03 Batch 0003

Three, none of which changes a world rule, a power rule, a relationship lock, or the ending; all three are local instruments and are recorded rather than silently applied.

1. **The four refused questions are now structural, not personal.** A room may not ask what a woman felt, whether it was right, where a thing came from, or who told her to do it, and the fen enters the count of the times a question has been available and not taken. This is the first instrument in this manuscript that governs what a body may *not* ask, and it is unenforceable in exactly the way the refusal clause is unenforceable, which is the manuscript's standing condition on all of its instruments.
2. **The second copy of a thing has to be a person and a person cannot be put on a return.** A new rule of custody, the counterpart of the fifth clause, and it is the first rule in this manuscript that requires an *unnameable* holder, which means it cannot be audited and which the fen has entered as a cost rather than as a safeguard.
3. **The mechanism as verification.** Marek Vale's standing that a row of eleven dressed cuts that came back is an instance and not an answer, and Tova Reed's that a mechanism is not a verification and soundness is not a hand, together make a rule the whole of Movement 4 runs on: **this body may say what a thing did and may not say who told it to do it, and may not cite the absence of a telling as either a comfort or a danger.**

## The Batch 0003 review pass — twenty-six findings, all closed, and what each one changed

**An independent review was run over Chapters 119–128 and over `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` and returned twenty-six findings. Every one is closed. The rules of the repair: preserve the prose, do not restart the batch, do not change the planned plot, and correct a figure rather than delete the sentence that carries it. No scene was replaced and no card was dropped.**

### Figures that were wrong on the page

1. **Ch 123 said the fragment was given on the ninth of this month.** It was given on the **nineteenth** (Ch 123:5, Ch 127:9, and the whole of the chapter). Corrected. The nineteenth is fixed and no chapter may re-date it.
2. **The boards stood at one hundred and forty-three on the thirtieth.** The rule is **day minus sixty-six**; day 210 − 66 = **one hundred and forty-four**. Corrected in Ch 128, in this file, in `open-threads.md` and in the Batch 0004 prompt, which had also slipped the day to the twenty-ninth. Ch 126's one hundred and forty on the twenty-sixth (day 206) is right and was not touched.
3. **Ch 128 said the seventh column moved by two in seventeen days.** It moved on the sixteenth and again on the thirtieth, **fourteen** days apart; the same line of the same chapter already said twenty-nine from the first. Corrected to fourteen.
4. **The second draw was undecided for eighteen days.** Ch 118 entered five days as of day 190, so day 210 is **twenty-five** days. Corrected in Ch 128 (twice), in this file and in `open-threads.md`. The Batch 0004 prompt's *a fortnight and six days* was the same error a third time and is now *twenty-five days*.
5. **The hired man was arithmetically impossible, and this was the only structural repair in the batch.** The road is **four days** — the fen's own standing, entered in Ch 121 and load-bearing for the whole shelf geography — and a round trip is eight days of it, so **a hire at four shillings a day is thirty-two shillings.** As drafted he cost eight, went up a *nine day* road, and came back four days after he was hired. **Repaired without moving a beat:** he is hired at the change of horses on the **twelfth**, four days before the figure he was sent for exists, which is a thing a body that plans a road would do; four shillings go in that day and twenty-four on the sixteenth; he comes off the change of horses on the **twentieth** as before, four days after the reading and on the four-day road; and the fen enters that **it did not have thirty-two shillings and took them out of the compost line anyway.** Corrected in Ch 121, Ch 122 (in the document, which is where the manuscript's own rule says documents are correct) and Ch 123. **The rate is now a lock on Batch 0004, where the same impossible eight shillings had been carried into three cards.**
6. **The same hire was entered on two different days.** The *day before he was hired* is gone with the whole clause; the hire is now entered in two parts on the days it was actually entered.
7. **The day letter was on two different days.** Ch 121 put the households' day at *the first of the month after next month*, which from the seventh month is **day 241**; Ch 116's letter, Ch 128 and the Batch 0004 prompt all put it on **day 211**. **Day 211 governs** and it is the one the four households named in a barn before this fen had a figure worth looking at. The prompt's own clock row carried the same slip and is corrected at source. Secondary: Ch 128 said the letter *ends* on the first, though it was entered on the sixth and runs a month; it now says the letter runs a month and that the households named the first to look at it.
8. **Ch 123 attributed four figures to the statement that the statement does not contain.** The hundred and forty miles, the seventy minutes, the twenty minutes, the four hours, the thirty-one miles and the ten weeks are in none of the nine lines. **Repaired as provenance rather than by deletion**, because the chapter's own device already had the room for it: Anneke Roos says at the top of the scene that she will use the figures the woman has said out loud and write *not held* where she has not got one, so the true position is that these six came **out of a mouth in the room on the seventeenth and were not written down.** They are now entered as hers and unwritten in the three places that use them, which makes the chapter's central claim — that the arithmetic has come down to a slate and a *not held* — tighter than it was, and the reader can now tell which numbers came off a page.
9. **Ch 126 said the man at the second house came down the road four days ago.** He came on the fifteenth and Ch 126 is the twenty-five. **Ten days.**

### Counts that did not reconcile

10. **The man of about seventy was brought out four times in the batch with two incompatible tallies.** He is now **fourth in a season on the fourteenth (Ch 120), fifth on the twenty-first (Ch 124), sixth on the twenty-fourth (Ch 125) and seventh on the twenty-seventh (Ch 127)**, and Ch 124's count is **the second time in a fortnight**, checkable against the fourteenth. Ch 109 of Batch 0002 also calls itself the fourth in a season and is untouched; the two do not collide inside this batch.
11. **Ch 125 said the speech was eleven sentences and the fen entered that she *had been asked* for them.** The speech is **thirteen**, the closing paragraph is **the last four of them** and not *the two sentences that are the rest of it*, and the entry is now that she **had not** been asked for them.

### Accounting and continuity that had no referent

12. **Twenty days against a fortnight's keep, and the six days were never entered by anybody.** **They are now entered on the thirtieth, in a document, at one shilling and sevenpence**, being six days at three shillings and eightpence for fourteen rounded up because the fen will not enter a fraction — **and with the reason they were not entered on the twenty-fourth: that on the twenty-fourth this body did not know she would still be in the yard on the thirtieth.** This is the batch's argument applied to its own housekeeping and it is the right place for it.
13. **Ch 128 said the bucket is a foot and a half shorter.** The entered figure is **three and a half feet against a man and half an inch against the ground**, and it is three and a half feet in both the tenses of that speech. Corrected.
14. **Ch 126's room inventory ended with a woman of about fifty-four's fruit in four crates in a corner of a yard four hundred miles off, and then said none of those people said anything.** Fruit is not a person and the yard is not the room, and the season was sold in Ch 117 so the fruit had no standing in this chapter either. **Replaced with the two people who are in fact in that room**, the engineer of record at the near end of the table and the seedwright by the door, which is what the list was reaching for.

### Prose, and one thing that is not prose

15. **Four duplicate sentences of ten words or more, all of them standing texts of this manuscript.** The door script, the *taken by nobody* entry and the *worth nothing against a cut* closing line are each read or written the same way every time by design, and the earlier claim in the state files that there were none but three door lines was false. **The three texts are kept and are now enumerated here with all their instances, and the mechanical claim in the state files is corrected rather than the prose.** This is recorded as a ruling so that the next pass does not strip them a second time.
16. **The word *eleven* at sixty in 29,179 words, clustered in Chs 122 and 125.** Three inert instances varied, all in Ch 123; **the remaining fifty-seven are each a different standing figure and are listed in the mechanical check above.** Ch 127's *three sentences in a fortnight* was also corrected to *two months*, which is an interval and not an eleven.

### The state files were wrong in four places and are now right

17. **The duplicate-sentence claim was false** and is corrected in `current.md` and above. **The claim that the words *became* and *redeemed* do not appear at all was false** — *became* is in Ch 127, in a sentence about a compliment turning into a standing — **and the prohibition is scoped to Kellan Rusk, which is what every earlier state file actually said.**
18. **The Quiet Break was described as a dated refusal.** It is a refusal **carrying an age**: *I was not at the Quiet Break. It is a hundred and twenty-one years old. I was not born.* **The card and lock 2 conflict and the card's wording won and the lock is amended**, because an age is the only form available to a woman who was not born and because *dated* would have set her in a year she has no business being in. It remains a number, an age, and no date, no region and no history.
19. **Brinewake is canon and is not on the page, and the ending needs it.** The review is right that the Volume 04 restitution beat has no name to land on. **The decision, now binding on every later writer:** the name stays out of Volume 03, entirely, in every medium — no document, no speech, no narration, no gloss — **and it is recovered in Volume 04 out of Sera Quill's own mouth, as the last and largest instance of the volume's central defect: a fact that exists in one uncorroborated memory, checkable by nobody including the woman who holds it, or in a sealed compensation record that does not open in this volume.** That is the shape of Chapter 99's question and the name now answers it instead of sitting in a glossary. **A later batch may not put the name in a document, may not have an office supply it, and may not have anybody say it before Volume 04.** The Batch 0004 prompt's blanket *No Brinewake* is replaced with this reservation.

### The next batch's prompt, seven defects, all repaired before dispatch

20. **Lock 4 forbade a second draw inside the batch while the clock table and Ch 135's card required one**, so a writer obeying the locks could not write the chapter. Lock 4 now permits **exactly one**, in Ch 135, and forbids any other.
21. **The man at the second house was scheduled and forbidden at once** — line 285 put him on the shelf on the eighteenth, line 293 called him four hundred miles off, and lock 11 said his return is not decided and is not invented. **The card's sentence is struck and lock 11 governs:** he is not in this fen and is not on the shelf in any narrated scene, and whether he is nine miles off or four hundred is not decided and is not narrated.
22. **Two cards sent a compost line to *the first of the ninth month, which is outside this volume*.** Day 241 is the first of the ninth and Batch 0005 opens on day 231, so Batch 0005 cannot stop before it. **Both now read *outside this batch*, and Batch 0005 is told it owes that line.**
23. **Two broken bold runs** at the compost-line card and the second-draw card, where the inner span never closed. Closed.
24. **Ch 130 replayed Ch 119's arrival beat for Sabin Roell**, who already came up the fen road, gave her name and was entered in Ch 107 and has already had a formal exchange with this body. The card now requires the beat to be **the opposite of it** — a records officer who has read the fourth part before she arrives, who does not give her name twice and is not entered by the clerk at a gate, and who works from a form and not from a person.
25. **Ch 131 asserted nine places with no enumeration anywhere, and Ch 133 said the sum gets worse while also pricing the second half at nothing and saying the sum cannot be finished.** The count is now **four and is enumerated** in the card and in the clerk's count in the chapter, and Ch 133's resistance now says that only the half that can be done is done and that neither half is finished, which is consistent with its own two-halves device.
26. **Ch 129 conflated two rates**, calling the messenger's hire *four shillings the hundredweight*. The hundredweight price is the compost valuation; the man is four shillings **a day**. Separated, and the impossible eight-shilling total is corrected to thirty-two throughout.

## Length and mechanical check on the drafted Batch 0003, after the review pass

**29,496 words across ten chapters by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, range 2,720–3,119.** Inside 27,000–30,000 and inside 2,550–3,120 at every chapter. **Ch 123 at 3,119 and Ch 119 at 3,118 are the two near a ceiling and a later writer must not lengthen either.** Ch 123 was drafted at 2,288 and was filled with scene and not with summary; Ch 124 came in at 2,720 and is the shortest in the batch and the batch as a whole was over the floor at every chapter on the final pass.

- **Quotation marks even in all ten files.** Four files were one mark out on the drafting pass, in the same way each time, and were corrected by removing a redundant opening mark at the last paragraph of a speech. **No word and no scene moved.**
- **No non-ASCII glyph at all**, so no curly mark and no em dash, in all ten files.
- **No month named** in narration or in any document. **October is seven months back by day 191 and is named nowhere.** The only capitalized *March* is *the March's tin*.
- **The word *seat* does not appear.** No Crown Engine, no Iona Vey, no False Season, no Thornwild, no Kellan Rusk. **The name *Brinewake* is not on the page, and that is a reservation with a Volume 04 landing rather than a standing prohibition — see the guardrails above and the review-pass section below.** **The Continuity Office is named once, on paper, in the statement of the seventeenth, and nowhere else in the batch.**
- **The Quiet Break appears once, in the ninth line of the statement, and it is a refusal carrying an age, not a dated refusal.** *I was not at the Quiet Break. It is a hundred and twenty-one years old. I was not born.* **A number, an age, no date, no region, no history, and no chapter may put any of those four back in.**
- **No British spellings.** A sweep for thirty forms over 29,496 words returns nothing.
- **Duplicate paragraphs and duplicate sentences of ten words or more: four pairs, and all four are standing texts of this manuscript. The earlier claim in this file — that there were none but three door lines in Chs 113 and 114 — was wrong in both particulars and is withdrawn.** They are: **(one)** three sentences of the well house door reading script, identical in **Ch 121 and Ch 128**, the same script standing in Chs 99, 108, 113 and 114 of Batches 0001 and 0002, where the fixed part of the list is read the same words every time and the live part, the compost line and the count, varies; **(two)** *It was taken by nobody and it is not going to be taken*, identical in **Ch 124 and Ch 126**, an entry this fen counts aloud, the count of three on the page in both, the same clause in Chs 107 and 113; **(three)** *An argument. This is worth nothing against a cut*, the closing line of a section-nine note, identical in **Chs 122, 124, 127, 128** and in seven chapters of Batches 0001 and 0002; **(four)** nothing else. **No reviewer should flag these three texts again; they are the manuscript's own formulas and a pass that strips them is a pass that is wrong.**
